Re³Rank：基于推理条件检索头的列表式重排序

9/10
Re$^3$Rank: Listwise Reranking with Reasoning-Conditioned Retrieval Heads
Ruiyi Yan, Zhuoyuan Mao, Yugo Murawaki, Yiwen Guo
核心总结:

针对固定问题作为查询信息有限的问题，提出Re³Rank：先用生成器写出问题与候选的推理轨迹，再用轨迹token作为选定检索头的注意力查询计算相关性分数，并通过直通估计器将排序损失回传至离散轨迹以联合训练生成器与打分器。

个性化推荐理由:

论文提出基于推理条件检索头的列表级重排序方法，直接针对排序核心任务，结合LLM推理与注意力机制，对搜索/推荐/广告排序有强借鉴意义。

2026-11-02 05:24:12 | arXiv:2611.02550v1 |
cs.CL
查看完整摘要
Attention-based rerankers score each candidate by the attention mass that retrieval heads route to it from the question, but the question, serving as a fixed query, carries limited information for locating relevant evidence. We propose Re$^3$Rank, a listwise reranker built on reasoning-conditioned retrieval heads (RRHeads). A generator first writes a reasoning trace over the question and the candidates, and a scorer then uses the trace tokens as the attention query of a few selected RRHeads, reading relevance as continuous scores. We present a straight-through estimator to propagate the ranking loss through the discrete trace, so that the generator and the scorer can be trained jointly for ranking. Averaged over 9 benchmarks, Re$^3$Rank outperforms 11 baseline rerankers: our 4B variant exceeds the strongest baseline by 3.11 NDCG@10 points on average, and even our 0.6B variant surpasses rerankers with up to 32B parameters.

面向生成式推荐的多样化教师在线策略蒸馏

8/10
Diverse-Teacher On-Policy Distillation for Generative Recommendation
Zhipeng Yi, Xinyu Lin, Minxuan Shan, Zipeng Chen, Yujiao Zhao, Chuanyu Xu, Wenji...
核心总结:

问题：生成式推荐的在线策略蒸馏(OPD)中，教师反馈与学生自回归采样双方都偏好热门物品SID，两个偏差相互强化导致推荐多样性下降。核心思路：HERO按真实物品热度将样本路由到热门/冷门两个异质教师分别蒸馏，并将冷门物品的真实SID注入训练以覆盖学生极少生成的SID路径，且仅用学生模型推理。

个性化推荐理由:

直接面向生成式推荐的核心训练方法，用在线策略蒸馏缓解流行度偏差以提升多样性，属于LLM技术在推荐中的直接创新应用。

2026-11-02 15:14:16 | arXiv:2611.03272v1 |
cs.IR
查看完整摘要
Generative recommendation generates the semantic ID (SID) of the next item from a user's interaction history. Existing training objectives mainly supervise target SID tokens, while on-policy distillation (OPD) provides denser supervision through a teacher's probability distribution over all tokens for more effective and efficient training. However, OPD can aggravate popularity bias: teacher feedback favors popular-item tokens, transferring this preference to the student, while student rollout favors popular items, leaving unpopular-item SID paths rarely generated and supervised. These two sources of bias reinforce each other and reduce recommendation diversity. We propose HERO, a diversity-aware OPD framework. Heterogeneous teacher distillation trains two teachers specializing in popular and unpopular items and routes each training sample to the teacher matching the popularity group of its ground-truth item. The student learns from the routed teacher's probability distribution along its own generated SIDs. Diversity-aware item rollout incorporates ground-truth SIDs of unpopular items into training, covering SID paths that the student rarely generates. Only the student is used for inference. Offline experiments on an industrial dataset and three public datasets further demonstrate its effectiveness, diversity, and robustness. Moreover, we deploy HERO in the "Guess What You Like" module on the 1688 homepage, where a 15-day online A/B test shows a 3.412% increase in gross merchandise value (GMV).

PACERec：基于协同证据的LLM物品嵌入渐进式适配用于推荐

8/10
PACERec: Progressive Adaptation of LLM Item Embeddings with Collaborative Evidence for Recommendation
Junzhao Huang, Xi Wu, Zihan Wang, Zijing Wang, Ming Wang, Kang Liu, Xiaocui Yang...
核心总结:

研究问题：如何在行为证据分布不均时，把频繁物品的前序交互上下文显式迁移到长尾物品，以适配LLM静态语义嵌入。核心方法：无ID的渐进适配框架，用频率加权流匹配从交互充足物品学习共享速度场，逐步将静态LLM嵌入导向协同行为目标，同时保留原始语义，实现序列转移模式向长尾物品的泛化。

个性化推荐理由:

该文将LLM物品嵌入通过频率加权流匹配适配到协同行为目标，直接面向序列推荐中长尾泛化问题，属LLM在推荐中的直接应用且含Transformer序列骨干验证，核心相关。

2026-11-02 12:55:49 | arXiv:2611.03068v1 |
cs.IRcs.AI
查看完整摘要
Large language model (LLM) embeddings have been increasingly adopted in sequential recommendation to encode static item attributes and alleviate the limitations of item ID embeddings under sparse interactions. However, these embeddings typically capture only static content similarity while neglecting sequential transitions, and since their adaptation quality relies on sparse per-item updates or global geometric calibration, existing methods still struggle to explicitly transfer preceding interaction contexts from frequent to long-tail items under unevenly distributed behavioral evidence, limiting recommendation effectiveness across the catalog. To address this challenge, we propose PACERec, an ID-free progressive adaptation framework that incorporates preceding interaction contexts into semantic representation adaptation by learning a shared velocity field from items with sufficient interactions via frequency-weighted flow matching. In this way, PACERec progressively guides static LLM embeddings toward collaborative behavioral targets while preserving original item semantics, overcoming sparse per-item updates to effectively generalize sequential transition patterns to long-tail items for both user modeling and candidate scoring. Extensive experiments on three real-world datasets across three sequential backbones demonstrate the superiority of our method over competitive baselines in both overall and long-tail recommendation.

学习裁决：验证器引导的多智能体推理用于电商相关性

8/10
Learning to Adjudicate: Verifier-Guided Multi-Agent Reasoning for E-Commerce Relevance
Shi Shu, Hao Chen, Baopu Qiu, Chao Wei, Weiru Zhang, Xiu Li, Xiaoyi Zeng
核心总结:

针对电商搜索中证据冲突导致难样本判定困难的问题，提出CADRE框架：三个专家智能体分别从产品属性、用户意图和业务规则独立分析，可训练验证器依据匹配准则评估证据，在专家分歧时触发验证器引导的辩论以澄清和修订解释，并通过多轮组相对策略优化与成功经验回放训练验证器，最终蒸馏为轻量学生模型用于在线服务。

个性化推荐理由:

论文提出多智能体辩论与验证器引导的电商相关性判定框架，直接应用于搜索/广告相关性建模，并涉及多轮强化学习与经验回放策略，与RecSys/Ads领域及LLM推理技术高度相关。

2026-11-02 08:39:57 | arXiv:2611.02758v1 |
cs.IR
查看完整摘要
Relevance modeling is fundamental to e-commerce search, determining how well retrieved products satisfy the needs expressed in a user's query. Accurate judgments require interpreting user intent, comparing product attributes, and applying business-specific matching criteria. Current methods based on large language models (LLMs) leverage contextual understanding and reasoning, focusing on incorporating domain-specific context and enhancing reasoning performance. Despite these advances, difficult query-product pairs remain challenging when evidence from different perspectives supports conflicting conclusions. A single reasoning pass may fail to reconcile these conflicts and ensure that decisive evidence guides the final judgment. To address this challenge, we propose Collaborative Adjudication with Debate and Replay Experience (CADRE), a framework for evidence-based adjudication in e-commerce relevance assessment. CADRE organizes three expert agents sharing a frozen backbone to analyze product attributes, user intent, and business rules in separate contexts, while a trainable Verifier assesses their supporting evidence against the applicable matching criteria to determine relevance. Under expert disagreement, a Verifier-guided debate elicits targeted clarification and revision of disputed interpretations before final adjudication. We optimize the Verifier through multi-turn group relative policy optimization and adapt successful-experience replay so that trajectories yielding correct terminal labels can be reused alongside fresh rollouts in subsequent updates. For efficient online serving, we use CADRE as an offline teacher to distill its relevance judgments into a lightweight student model. Offline evaluations and online A/B tests on an e-commerce search advertising platform demonstrate that CADRE improves relevance classification and key business and relevance satisfaction metrics.

面向LLM推荐的多教师推理蒸馏与自探索

8/10
Multi-Teacher Reasoning Distillation for LLM Recommendation with Self-Exploration
Liangbo Ning, Siyuan Liang, Yi Zhou, Yifeng Zhang, Qing Li, Wenqi Fan, Dacheng T...
核心总结:

针对现有单教师推理蒸馏受限于教师知识天花板且不加区分利用推理轨迹的问题，提出MUSE框架：构建多教师推理池并通过学生自探索扩充，设计Rank-Gain效用评估筛选对推荐有益的轨迹对，结合双层对比学习同时优化推理生成与推荐编码器。

个性化推荐理由:

该论文直接针对LLM推荐系统中的推理蒸馏，提出多教师选择和自探索框架，属于LLM for RecSys的直接应用，核心贡献与推荐领域高度相关。

2026-11-02 03:20:28 | arXiv:2611.02457v1 |
cs.IRcs.AI
查看完整摘要
Reasoning-augmented recommender systems (RecSys) enhance user preference modeling by generating reasoning trajectories over historical interactions before making recommendations. Knowledge distillation provides a practical paradigm for transferring such reasoning capabilities from powerful large language models to lightweight RecSys. However, existing methods typically rely on a single fixed teacher, remain constrained by the teacher knowledge ceiling, and indiscriminately distill teacher-generated trajectories without assessing their actual utility for downstream recommendation. To address these limitations, we propose MUSE (Multi-Teacher Utility-guided Selection with Self-Exploration), an adaptive reasoning distillation framework that jointly improves reasoning generation and reasoning utilization. MUSE first constructs a diverse reasoning pool using multiple teacher LLMs and progressively augments it via student self-exploration, enabling the student to leverage the strengths of different teachers and gradually explore reasoning strategies beyond teacher imitation. We then develop Rank-Gain Utility Assessment (RGUA), which evaluates each candidate according to the normalized full-catalog rank improvement of the ground-truth item and identifies the most and least recommendation-beneficial trajectories. Based on these utility-aware trajectory pairs, we introduce dual-level contrastive learning objectives that guide the student generator to prefer beneficial reasoning while training the recommendation encoder to more effectively exploit recommendation-relevant preference evidence. Extensive experiments on three real-world datasets demonstrate the effectiveness and robustness of the proposed framework.

逐句皆重要：通过分解为多个小规模、可回答问题的指令遵循重排序

7/10
Every Clause Counts: Instruction-Following Reranking by Multiplying Small, Answerable Questions
S. M. Mahbub Uddin Ahmed, Abdullah Al Musaddiq
核心总结:

针对搜索重排序器将用户附加指令仅视为额外关键词、指令遵循能力差的问题，提出DMReranker：将带指令查询拆分为主题与若干类型化子句，用小判断模型对每个子句回答一个窄问题，将各答案概率相乘作为重排序分数，无需训练。

个性化推荐理由:

提出零样本重排序器，将指令查询分解为子句并用小模型逐条判断概率相乘，直接解决搜索中指令遵循重排序问题，属于LLM在搜索重排序的直接应用。

2026-11-02 14:15:23 | arXiv:2611.03179v1 |
cs.IRcs.CL
查看完整摘要
People often attach instructions to their search queries, asking for pages written for beginners, documents in a certain language or official sources only. Most rerankers were trained to judge topical relevance and treat such instructions as a few extra words. On the InfoSearch benchmark, even GPT-4o used as a list-wise reranker reaches an average WISE (Weighted Instruction Sensitivity Evaluation) of only 33.5. We present DMReranker (Decision-Model Reranker), a zero-shot reranker that splits an instructed query into a topic and a short list of typed clauses. A small judge model answers one narrow question per clause, and the answer probabilities are multiplied into a single score. Nothing is trained, and the configuration was frozen before the test runs. Reranking the same top-100 candidates as the published baselines, DMReranker reaches a macro WISE of 50.9 over all six InfoSearch dimensions while keeping original-mode nDCG@10 at 82.9. It trails GPT-4o on the stricter SICR (Strict Instruction Compliance Ratio) metric, 19.2 against 32.1. We show that this gap follows from how a product of probabilities behaves when SICR compares raw scores across runs. Computed from ranks, our SICR is 67.0. On the same judge, splitting the instruction raises WISE from 32.9 to 47.6 over a single combined question, while raw-score SICR falls. Scoring all six dimensions cost about $10 at list price.

E-GenRM：面向开放式电商购物智能体的生成式奖励建模

7/10
E-GenRM: Generative Reward Modeling for Open-Ended E-Commerce Shopping Agents
Ying Sun, Qiuyu Zhao, Xiao Wang, Xiyu Zhao, Gege Shi, Zenghui Sun, Jinsong Lan, ...

2026-11-02 12:10:57 | arXiv:2611.03008v1 |
cs.IR
查看完整摘要
We present E-GenRM (E-commerce Generative Reward Model), a generative reward model tailored to e-commerce shopping agents. User expectations are shifting from simple product queries to open-ended shopping tasks. However, existing trajectories and reward designs remain largely bounded by current agent usage patterns and known failures, providing limited guidance for further capability improvement. These limitations call for an integrated reward modeling framework for open-ended shopping agent tasks. On the data side, E-GenRM leverages naturally occurring user behavior across the shopping app to construct diverse, long-horizon conversational trajectories beyond established usage patterns. On the modeling side, E-GenRM first learns from behavior-grounded evaluation chains through supervised fine-tuning. It is then optimized through criterion-guided reinforcement learning, which rewards correct preference judgments and evidence-supported factual claims under each criterion. Personalized hard-negative training further improves its sensitivity to user preferences reflected in shopping behavior, beyond explicit requirements. We additionally develop an active evidence retrieval extension that acquires missing e-commerce information for evaluation. On E-GenRM-Bench, E-GenRM-27B achieves 87.40\% preference accuracy, outperforming the strongest baseline by 6.97 percentage points. In a 14-day online A/B test on Taobao, a shopping agent post-trained with E-GenRM improves the effective shopping-assistance rate by 1.39 percentage points and user satisfaction by 2.33 percentage points.

RecRSI: 机制引导的推荐算法发现

7/10
RecRSI: Mechanism-Guided Discovery of Recommendation Algorithms
Tingran Gan, Zhouziyue Lu, Dian Jin, Tao Li, Yang Gao, Ming Liu, Weiwen Liu, Jia...
核心总结:

针对推荐算法设计中交互设计选择难以协调与实验学习的问题，论文提出RecRSI框架，用机制假设将机制变更与参考设计、理由和预期观察关联，并通过MechSpace生成原语、Research Line发展假设、Mechanism Feedback回传证据，使每次实验指导下一步机制开发与组合。

个性化推荐理由:

该论文提出基于LLM的推荐算法自动发现框架，直接属于LLM在推荐系统中的应用，且机制假设驱动的研究流程对搜索/广告算法设计亦有启发。

2026-11-02 04:02:13 | arXiv:2611.02487v1 |
cs.IRcs.AI
查看完整摘要
Improving recommendation algorithms for the Web requires coordinating interacting design choices and learning from experiments. We present RecRSI, an LLM-based research framework that uses mechanism hypotheses to organize algorithm discovery. Each hypothesis links a proposed mechanism change to a reference design, a rationale, and an expected observation. MechSpace derives implementable primitives and composition rules from the task source; Research Line uses this space and prior evidence to develop and test hypotheses; Mechanism Feedback carries outcomes and their design context into subsequent research. This organization makes each experiment inform which mechanisms to develop, revise, or combine next. Across four initial recommenders and four datasets, RecRSI yields average relative improvements of 8.48% in NDCG@5 and 6.88% in HR@5 over the initial algorithms, and 2.60% and 2.20%, respectively, over the strongest competing result for each setting and metric. Component and process studies link these gains to coordinated mechanism choices and evidence-guided follow-up. Our code is publicly available at https://github.com/DynamisG210/RecRSI.

微调数据中的预处理选择至关重要：基于LLM的推荐中的效用与人口统计学敏感性

6/10
Preprocessing Choices in Fine-Tuning Data Matter: Utility and Demographic Sensitivity in LLM-Based Recommendation
Furkan Yasir Goksu, Yaren Yilmaz, Sule Gunduz Oguducu
核心总结:

研究生成式LLM推荐中微调数据预处理（物品标题表示、人口平衡、知识增强）如何影响推荐效用与公平性；核心结论是这些预处理选择需在效用、输出行为与人口敏感性上联合评估，且公平性效果依赖具体属性。

个性化推荐理由:

研究LLM生成式推荐中微调数据预处理对效用与人口敏感性的影响，属直接LLM应用但更偏实证分析，未涉及架构或VLM类比创新。

2026-11-02 13:32:28 | arXiv:2611.03113v1 |
cs.IRcs.CLcs.LG
查看完整摘要
Large language models (LLMs) have introduced a generative approach to recommendation, producing item lists from textual representations of user histories and item information. However, this generative setting raises challenges concerning output validity, prompt consistency, and sensitivity to demographic descriptors. In this work, we investigate how preprocessing choices in fine-tuning data affect recommendation utility and fairness-related behavior in a generative LLM-based recommender system. We first establish a controlled recommendation pipeline that combines supervised fine-tuning, catalog-constrained decoding, and prompt augmentation. We then study three preprocessing choices: item-title representation, demographic balancing, and auxiliary item-knowledge enrichment. Experiments on MovieLens-1M show that the catalog-constrained pipeline eliminates out-of-catalog items and ensures complete ten-item outputs, while prompt augmentation improves prompt consistency and utility. Original MovieLens titles increase HR@10 from 10.67% to 12.47% and Coverage@10 from 41.36% to 47.88%. Fairness-related effects are attribute-dependent: age balancing and knowledge enrichment reduce selected group-level utility gaps while largely preserving utility, whereas gender balancing reduces utility without improving the evaluated gender-related indicators. Overall, preprocessing choices in fine-tuning data should be evaluated jointly in terms of utility, output behavior, and demographic sensitivity.

LLM智能体能否成为前向部署工程师？企业级Text-to-SQL的需求发现与交付

6/10
Can LLM Agents Be Forward-Deployed Engineers? Requirement Discovery and Delivery for Enterprise Text-to-SQL
Marianne Menglin Liu, Rongguang Wang, Zheng Wang, Yewen Wang, Leonid Boytsov, Ta...
核心总结:

研究问题：LLM编码agent能否充当FDE，在模糊的企业Text-to-SQL需求中自主发现验收标准/权限/硬件约束并规划执行post-training交付；核心方法是通过模拟真实客户的多阶段交互实验，识别需求发现不全、执行不可靠、算力预算未用足、基准精度不等于生产可用四大瓶颈，并提出人工审查需求、执行中监控计划与显式实验环境规范。

个性化推荐理由:

2026-11-02 18:13:38 | arXiv:2611.03551v1 |
cs.CLcs.AI
查看完整摘要
LLM coding agents can increasingly automate model post-training, but whether they can act as forward-deployed engineer (FDE) agents, discovering customer requirements and turning them into deployment decisions, remains unclear. We study this on a real enterprise text-to-SQL engagement: a simulated customer, grounded in the real use case's requirements, starts with an ambiguous request. An agent must discover acceptance criteria, data permissions, and hardware constraints in the discovery phase, then plan and execute a post-training recipe in the delivery phase. Across 15 models, the strongest discover over 80% of withheld requirements and open-weight models discover 40-50%; in the delivery phase, only 25% of runs across eight coding-agent configurations pass all acceptance criteria. We identify four bottlenecks in this engagement: (1) incomplete discovery, with 40% of withheld requirements missing or misrepresented; (2) unreliable execution; (3) underused compute budgets and a lack of systematic comparisons across base models and post-training methods; and (4) benchmark accuracy does not guarantee production readiness. We suggest human review of discovered requirements, monitoring of the agent's plan during execution, and an explicit experiment-environment specification covering hardware, budget scope, and production checks.

当奖励取决于群体时，GRPO 进行锦标赛

6/10
When Rewards Depend on the Group, GRPO Plays a Tournament
Junyi Gao, Yu Shi, Pingzhao Hu, Ewen M Harrison
核心总结:

问题：GRPO/RLOO优化多样性、覆盖度等组依赖奖励时，组相对基线会改变优化目标。核心思路：证明GRPO等价于零和锦标赛（α=-1/(K-1)），提出α=+1的Team-GRPO作为无偏估计，无需额外模型调用即可支持两样本训练的成对奖励。

个性化推荐理由:

该文剖析了GRPO在组依赖奖励下的有偏性问题并提出Team-GRPO修正，属于LLM对齐训练的核心算法进展，对RecSys中基于GRPO的多样性优化有直接方法借鉴价值。

2026-11-02 02:26:46 | arXiv:2611.02404v1 |
cs.LGcs.CL
查看完整摘要
Rewards for diversity, coverage and distribution matching make the reward of each sampled response depend on the rest of its group, and they are now routinely optimized with GRPO and RLOO. We prove that in this setting the group-relative baseline changes the objective. The expected update equals each response's own reward gradient plus $α$ times its effect on its groupmates' rewards, with $α=-1/(K-1)$ for groups of size $K$, so GRPO trains every response to beat its groupmates in a zero-sum tournament. It cannot see a pairwise diversity bonus when K=2, sees less than half of it at any K, and often does worse than using no baseline at all. Setting $α=+1$ gives Team-GRPO, an unbiased estimator for any group-dependent reward that works with two samples per prompt and requires no extra model calls for pairwise rewards. The theory predicts training quantitatively. It locates the fixed points of a 9B policy to within 0.02 in $\ell_1$ and predicts how concentrated trained policies become with Pearson r=0.995. On GlobalOpinionQA, GRPO collapses onto the majority answer and ends further from human opinion than the untrained model, while Team-GRPO cuts the error from $0.20$ to $0.08$. On NoveltyBench, Team-GRPO avoids the diversity collapse of GRPO and significantly improves set utility.

融合以对齐：基于超图正则化的多模态表示学习用于检索

6/10
Fuse To Align: Multimodal Representation Learning for Retrieval via Hypergraph Regularization
Anindya Nag, Ambuj Mehrish, Sebastiano Vascon
核心总结:

cs.CV
查看完整摘要
Multimodal retrieval must integrate complementary evidence from views that may be missing, noisy, or unequally informative. When a sample provides limited reliable evidence, learning from its views alone can hinder the acquisition of useful retrieval representations. Semantically related training samples may provide complementary observations, yet existing volume-based objectives jointly align modalities within each sample without explicitly enabling information exchange across samples. We therefore propose a training-time hypergraph fusion framework that connects observed modalities both within samples and across semantically related samples, allowing representation learning to draw on evidence beyond an individual sample while leaving the retrieval pipeline unchanged at inference. The framework includes HyVol as a special case and introduces two configurations: wHyVol learns view contribution weights and symmetric semantic affinities, whereas wRHG additionally models typed interactions and participant roles through relational propagation. Missing modalities are omitted rather than imputed, and the hypergraph is discarded after training, incurring no additional inference cost. Experiments on eleven zero-shot video-text and audio-text retrieval benchmarks demonstrate broad improvements with both GRAM and HyperGRAM. Across 17 video benchmark-modality settings evaluated in both retrieval directions, wRHG outperforms GRAM in all 34 comparisons and HyperGRAM in 33 of 34. Mean text-to-video R@1 increases from 63.4 to 68.2 with GRAM and from 64.8 to 68.4 with HyperGRAM, while the largest video-to-text gain reaches 13.3 percentage points on ActivityNet. Under missing-modality evaluation, both weighted configurations retain positive text-to-video gains over GRAM across seven benchmarks at every evaluated missingness rate, even when 90% of clips lack one modality.

2026-11-02 07:13:14 | arXiv:2611.02651v1 |
cs.CVcs.AI
查看完整摘要
Jointly trained multimodal networks frequently underutilize weaker modalities, as faster-learning modalities dominate the shared fusion objective and suppress gradients reaching slower ones. Most existing approaches throttle dominant modalities, whereas directly boosting weaker modalities often fails because monitoring signals derived from the joint loss remain dominated by stronger modalities. We propose Probe-Guided Gradient Boosting (PGGB), a gradient modulation approach that boosts weaker modalities while preserving the strength of dominant ones. PGGB attaches lightweight linear classifiers to stop-gradient features from each modality to estimate representation quality independently. The gap between these scores, termed the utilization gap, provides an imbalance signal used to adaptively scale weaker-modality gradients through bounded and smoothed modulation. PGGB is composable with throttling methods, as it leaves the loss function and fusion architecture unchanged. Experiments across eight benchmarks spanning four domains and 2-4 modalities demonstrate that PGGB outperforms state-of-the-art methods on highly imbalanced multimodal datasets while remaining competitive under low or negligible imbalance. We further establish bounded scaling, self-attenuation, and a descent bound for standard SGD under smoothness and bounded-variance assumptions. Code is available at https://github.com/VuVoCodes/PGGB.

Sonar-MoE：面向内存高效混合专家训练的反向传播内专家更新

Sonar-MoE: In-Backward Expert Updates for Memory-Efficient Mixture-of-Experts Training
Avni Mittal, Avinash Anand, Ashutosh Kumar, Dikshant Kukreja, Kritarth Prasad, C...
核心总结:

个性化推荐理由:

2026-11-02 18:30:00 | arXiv:2611.03577v1 |
cs.LGcs.AIcs.CLcs.PF
查看完整摘要
Most parameters of a Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) model belong to its experts, and standard training keeps every expert gradient in GPU memory until the optimizer step. This memory grows with the number and size of the experts, so it rises as MoE models scale. We introduce Sonar-MoE, which updates each expert tensor during the backward pass and frees its gradient right away. It brings optimizer-in-backward from dense models to batched MoE expert tensors, so the GPU never holds more than one layer of expert gradients. When we fine-tune OLMoE-1B-7B and Granite-3.1-3B-A800M in bf16 with two expert-kernel backends, Sonar-MoE lowers peak memory by 19% to 22%. Its updates are bitwise identical to standard training without expert gradient clipping, and held-out loss stays within 0.003 of all baselines. Each step also takes 17% to 23% less time than standard training with global-norm clipping. Updating whole batched tensors rather than individual experts is what keeps Sonar-MoE fast, as it avoids a 20% to 72% slowdown while using the same memory. Sonar-MoE matches optimizer-in-backward over all parameters in memory and speed while still clipping the dense parameters.

cs.CL
查看完整摘要
Scaling depth is a key route to stronger large language models, but Pre-LN architectures often suffer from depth-wise information dilution, limiting the effective use of deep layers. While cross-layer connectivity has proven effective at mitigating this problem, existing designs usually introduce additional computation or memory access. We revisit cross-layer KV sharing as an efficient form of such connectivity. Beyond cache compression, each KV reuse edge creates a direct gradient path from deep layers to source KV projections, turning KV sharing into an implicit structural regularizer for KV representation geometry. We provide a systematic analysis of this mechanism, showing that proper sharing topologies sharpen key retrieval directions while enriching the value content subspace, whereas existing adjacent or global-anchor designs do not fully exploit this effect. Motivated by this, we propose DepthGraft, a hierarchical KV-sharing architecture that reconstructs deep-layer KV from stride-aligned shallow-middle source pairs. DepthGraft distributes non-local gradient flow across source layers, while preserving prefill early completion and reducing KV cache by roughly one third. Experiments on dense and MoE models from 0.5B to 16B parameters show consistent downstream improvements under both GQA and MLA. Notably, on the 16B MoE model, DepthGraft achieves a 7.0% absolute improvement on MMLU-Pro, along with gains of 6.6% on CMMLU and 6.4% on C3. These results validate the potential of DepthGraft as a scalable design for large-scale pretraining. The project page is available at https://github.com/jianke0604/DepthGraft.

cs.LGcs.AIcs.CL
查看完整摘要
Computer programmers chain together the same reusable computational primitives, for example, conditional statements or loops, but in different orders to implement different algorithms. In contrast, existing language model architectures always execute their layers in the same order. We introduce Library of Layers (LoL), an architecture that dynamically orders its layers during inference to solve the problem at hand. LoL has a single hyperparameter, the size of its global library of transformer blocks, that allows it to transition seamlessly between parameter reuse and sparsity, generalizing the parameter-efficiency and FLOPs-efficiency tradeoffs of dense, depth-recurrent, and mixture of experts architectures in one unified family. We show that LoL achieves competitive performance with all three above baselines when pretrained on text data. On synthetic sudoku and maze data, we observe that the model employs distinct layer orderings to implement different solution strategies, and we can intervene on routing to force a model to apply a specific solution strategy to a problem instance. In a multilingual natural language setting, we find that LoL chooses different layer order patterns based on language and script. Similarly, we train LoL on safety data and fit probes to its router outputs, finding that these probes distinguish safe from unsafe text with high AUROC. LoL provides a path toward a future in which models dynamically compose their own computational graph, enabling more efficient and controllable reasoning.

查看完整摘要
Language models in customer-facing products must be continually updated, since the data they serve shifts over time. As retraining on the full accumulated data is impractical, each update should instead run on a small set of examples carrying the training signal of all data seen so far. The strongest current methods build this set by gradient matching, which synthesizes examples whose training gradients match those of the full data. However, they require ground-truth labels, run once on a fixed snapshot rather than on a growing stream, and are slow on large data. We introduce \textbf{OLGA}, which lifts these limitations. It distills unlabeled data by drawing the gradient matching targets from the model's own next-token predictions. Across five text classification benchmarks with phi-1.5, it improves accuracy by 10 percentage points over the strongest selection baseline using as few as five examples. OLGA also distills online data streams by keeping a running rehearsal memory of synthetic examples, which is re-distilled together with each new batch as it arrives. In the online setting, it outperforms the strongest selection-based continual baseline by 6.6 stream-AUC percentage points. Finally, our method scales to large data by pairing distillation with selection in a hybrid pipeline, letting selection handle the mild stage of compression and distillation the extreme one. It cuts runtime by 30\% versus distillation alone at similar accuracy, and beats the best selection-only pipeline by up to 12.6 percentage points.

cs.CL
查看完整摘要
This paper studies retrieval-augmented generation (RAG), which leverages external knowledge to improve the performance of language models. Despite the remarkable progress, current RAG approaches are still far from satisfactory due to inadequate retrieval and potential hallucinations. Towards this end, we propose a novel approach named Task Refinement with Individual Optimization (TRIO) for RAG. The core idea of our TRIO is to equip three well-designed agents with an iterative refinement paradigm by aligning their individual optimization objectives. Specifically, we first expand ambiguous queries with a query extension agent and quantify the reward based on the improved answer quality, which can be utilized to refine our agent for higher expansion efficiency. To enhance the retrieval quality, we include a unanimity voting to annotate the expanded query as insufficient or sufficient, and their generative outcomes are utilized as ground truth to optimize the judge agent. To further improve evidence utilization and answer accuracy, an answer agent is optimized with dynamic matching-based rewards with curriculum learning for final outputs. Extensive experiments across multiple benchmark datasets validate the effectiveness of the proposed TRIO in comparison with state-of-the-art approaches. Our code is available at https://github.com/yingtaoren/TRIO.

2026-11-02 07:00:18 | arXiv:2611.02637v1 |
cs.AIcs.CLcs.LG
查看完整摘要
Large language models (LLMs) have achieved strong performance across diverse tasks, from question answering to code generation. Recent studies have found that LLMs can perform numerical prediction on tabular datasets via in-context learning. However, it remains unclear under what conditions and how LLMs use variable names and in-context examples for numerical prediction. Here we show that semantic grounding, the match between variable names and the values they describe, allows five LLMs without task-specific training to match or outperform seven supervised machine learning (ML) models across 20 datasets from nine scientific domains, particularly when examples are scarce. Reasoning traces indicate that variable names allow LLMs to draw on domain-specific knowledge acquired during pretraining. Using controlled tasks based on physical formulas, we further show that LLMs integrate domain-specific knowledge with in-context examples in a way consistent with Bayesian updating. Specifically, when the relationship between the inputs and the target cannot be inferred from the examples alone, LLMs rely on domain-specific knowledge as the prior. Conversely, as contradicting examples accumulate, LLM predictions shift progressively towards these examples, which act as the likelihood. Our work reveals how LLMs use domain-specific knowledge in numerical prediction and positions them as data-efficient predictors for data-scarce scientific domains.

查看完整摘要
Scaled weight decay for Muon (Muon-SW) has been reported to train language models more efficiently than constant decoupled weight decay, but such comparisons usually reuse one learning-rate (LR) and weight-decay (WD) setting for both methods. We re-run the comparison under the MAI-Base-2 protocol: LR and WD are tuned jointly through their product for each method, on a source-scaled mixture-of-experts (MoE) scaling ladder (1.9M to 15M active parameters) that holds tokens per active parameter and data repetition fixed across sizes. We find: (1) Most of the gain is a tuning gap. At the shared setting Muon-SW wins by 0.035 bits per byte (BPB), a 1.52x compute saving; after both methods are tuned, the gap is 0.0026 BPB (95% CI [0.0018, 0.0034]), a 1.03x saving, so about 90% of the advantage disappears. (2) The small advantage that remains is real: at the smallest size, all four paired seeds favour Muon-SW at the tuned settings. (3) That advantage grows with model size: across an 8x range of active parameters, the tuned gap rises from 0.0036 to 0.0051 BPB while the untuned gap shrinks from 0.035 to 0.024, so the surviving share rises from about 10% to 21%. (4) The best weight decay moves with scale: the optimal LRWD product grows for both methods, faster for Muon-SW (ratio 1.40 to 1.91), and a law fitted on the two smallest sizes overshoots the third by 1.3-1.5x, costing up to 0.002 BPB. The whole study runs on one NVIDIA T4 GPU; we release the code and all run results.

Multi-teacher on-policy distillation (MOPD) distills domain experts into one student but needs every expert at once. When domains arrive one at a time and earlier experts are gone, the previous-stage student can teach replayed prompts of earlier domains inside every stage. Some pipelines instead store every stage snapshot for a final multi-teacher recovery stage. On five domains with a 1.5B student and five public experts, this paper compares both with joint MOPD. It varies the replay budget and the replay signal. The signal is token-level distillation alone or with alignment of hidden states, attention and value relations. Sequential distillation without replay or recovery is 6.2 points below 208-step joint MOPD on a 14-benchmark average and 3.5 points below on the 11 benchmarks outside safety. Without snapshots, replay toward the previous student closes 3.8 of the 6.2 points when the earlier domains share the replay budget of a stage. With a fixed share of seven replay steps per earlier domain and stage, it closes 5.8 over two training runs. This is level with the 5.9 of recovery from all snapshots, both at its default length and when given as many training steps as the fixed share. Alignment terms on replay steps change the average by about one point or less. If refusal and compliance count equally in safety, shared-budget replay ranks first among the trained configurations, and the fixed share stays at or above recovery. Against the shared budget, the fixed share lowers the score on the newest domain. Recovery lowers this score as well, in five of six cases. The replay steps of the fixed share grow quadratically with the number of stages.

Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) extends large language models with external evidence, yet relevant evidence does not ensure that a generator can use it. Indiscriminate retrieval can degrade task performance while adding retrieval and long-context inference costs. Existing methods neither determine before large-scale execution whether a generator, task distribution, and retriever configuration is worth deploying nor diagnose multiple candidate retrievers under a shared budget. We introduce EviCast, a framework that forecasts retrieval value through interventional evidence-state decomposition. EviCast measures a generator's response to controlled evidence states once and combines this shared profile with the offline evidence-state distribution of each candidate retriever, yielding the end-to-end net utility and its 95% lower confidence bound without executing every RAG configuration. A state-refinement analysis characterizes when its binary and topology-aware instantiations are exact and when shared calibration is statistically more efficient than per-retriever calibration. In frozen evaluations, EviCast correctly decides all 14 HotpotQA retrieval configurations with a forecast error of 2.75 F1 points while reducing diagnostic generation calls by 80%, and it attains the lowest forecast error among seven predictors in the low-budget regime. Its topology-aware instantiation, EviCast-T, captures the complementarity between terminal and bridge evidence, correctly deciding all 16 configurations on MuSiQue and HotpotQA and 23 of 24 after prospective transfer to a third dataset. These results show that retrieval deployment depends on both evidence availability and the generator's ability to convert evidence structure into stable net utility. Code is available at https://github.com/YiboWang2002/EviCast.

An image that is hard to classify on its own may have a helpful neighbor in the same unlabeled batch. Zero-shot vision-language models (VLMs) score images independently and leave this evidence unused. We study small unlabeled query batches with no labeled examples. Visual similarity can reflect pose or background, so it may connect images from different classes. Each image's scores over the class vocabulary offer another way to choose neighbors. We introduce Behavioral Affinity Transduction (BAT), which builds a sparse graph from these class-response profiles. The graph supplies neighbor evidence during prediction. For EM-Dirichlet, it also screens candidate classes and initializes assignments. BAT plugs into direct and iterative readouts of a frozen VLM. Across diverse datasets, BAT improves the mean accuracy of every tested readout. In the main RN50 comparison, soft and hard EM-Dirichlet gain $4.22$ and $2.72$ percentage points.

Complex documents such as scientific papers contain rich information in tables, figures, and equations; however, existing deep research (DR) agents typically reduce papers to OCR-extracted text. We instead ask whether vision-language models (VLMs) can enable page-native deep research: directly retrieving and reasoning over rendered document page images. We introduce DR Pixel, the first open-source end-to-end multimodal DR agent that uses rendered page images as its sole retrieval and reasoning unit, operating over a corpus of 1.06M pages. To evaluate this setting, we construct DRPixelBench, a benchmark on scientific papers requiring reasoning over text, figures, tables, and equations. Surprisingly, page-native DR already works well without training, outperforming OCR-based baselines across state-of-the-art models like GPT-5.4, Gemini-3.5-Flash, and Qwen3.6-27B, while requiring fewer tool calls. We next turn to DR training. A key challenge is that teacher trajectories for agentic supervised finetuning are noisy, mixing critical reasoning and action steps with redundant or failed attempts. To address this, we introduce counterfactual credit reweighting, a fine-grained credit assignment method that weights individual reasoning and action steps by their counterfactual contribution to the final answer. Our training improves retrieval recall by about 15 points and final accuracy by over 10 points on DRPixelBench, and likewise outperforms the untrained baseline on four existing benchmarks. On out-of-distribution ViDoRe-V3, DR Pixel comes within 1.8 points of an oracle that is given the gold pages directly. Our results suggest that the conventional OCR-then-retrieve pipeline is not only unnecessary but suboptimal: directly retrieving and reasoning over visual document pages makes complex DR both more effective and more efficient.

An indirect prompt injection can affect a search-augmented large language model only after its carrier page reaches model context. SearchInject controls this admission decision with a joint score combining retrieval relevance, search-abuse cues, instruction likelihood, and two relevance interactions. A quarantine threshold is selected under an empirical 0.5% clean-candidate false-quarantine constraint on a calibration split. Evaluation uses 300 held-out SciFact queries and held-out payload categories within four construction families already represented during development. The evidence supports retrieval exposure control within the evaluated construction families; the benefit of the interaction terms at matched clean cost remains unresolved.

查看完整摘要
A model is only half of an AI agent system. The other half is the inference strategy: the reasoning procedure that turns model calls into a response. Today that second half is written by hand, and the experience of building one strategy never carries to the next. We introduce Strategist, a meta-agent that evolves inference strategies across tasks. Each strategy is written as a blueprint,executable code that other strategies can reuse, and together the blueprints form a library. Given a task and a small development set, Strategist retrieves related blueprints, writes candidate strategies, tests them on real samples, and revises them from the results. Large language models (LLMs) show growing promise for scientific discovery, yet basic questions remain about what enables reliable discovery. How can we trace the scientific discovery capacity of LLMs to their training data? We study this question as one of missing probability mass and develop a theoretical account in which monofacts-the facts observed once during training-set the discovery capacity. In materials science and regulatory genomics, we directly manipulate monofact rates in training sets and find that more monofacts consistently yield more discoveries across two open-weight model families, sampling strategies, and reasoning budgets. Further experiments show the relationship cannot be explained by training-set diversity alone and can transfer across scientific domains when paired with in-domain data. Together, our results identify monofact rate in training data as a key factor governing discoveries in language models.

2026-11-02 14:25:14 | arXiv:2611.03198v1 |
cs.CLcs.AI
查看完整摘要
When a language model answers a yes/no question, that answer changes how it answers a different question about the same entity. We study this \emph{self-commitment} in 22 open-weight models from four families (0.5B--72B parameters), fixing the model's first answer and measuring the shift in its second across 3,600 question pairs about 120 entities. Each answer writes a \emph{commitment direction} into the residual stream. The similarity of two questions' directions predicts their coupling on held-out entities ($ρ= 0.43$--$0.85$), and ablating a single direction shared across topics leaves at most 17\% of the effect in every model. How this direction is read is far less stable: the reading circuit spreads across more attention heads as models grow, instruction tuning makes more questions read it in the opposite sense, and in one model changing only the system prompt reverses the circuit's sign. Ablating the direction while sparing the attention sink removes most of the coupling at under 1.3\% perplexity cost in 18 of 19 instruction-tuned models, though it shifts standalone answers in two. Without the sink spared, the direction's alignment with the leading principal component of ordinary text predicts which models pay a high cost. Written once, the commitment is read differently across scale, training and prompt.

2026-11-02 13:45:23 | arXiv:2611.03133v1 |
cs.CL
查看完整摘要
On-policy distillation (OPD) mitigates the student's exposure bias by optimizing on student-generated trajectories. However, student-generated prefixes may contain reasoning errors or follow patterns unfamiliar to the teacher, undermining the reliability of its token-level supervision. This degradation becomes more pronounced as prefixes grow longer. To address this teacher-side distribution mismatch, we propose Student-Prefix Teacher Adaptation (SPTA), which adapts the teacher to the states on which it is queried during distillation. Specifically, before OPD starts, we collect student rollout prefixes with random lengths and continually train the teacher to continue from the prefixes using reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR). By treating each prefix as context and rewarding correct final answers, the teacher learns to recover from student-induced states rather than imitate the student's reasoning. Then, the adapted teacher is used for standard OPD, with no changes to the distillation objective. Experiments on mathematical reasoning benchmarks show that our SPTA improves average student performance over standard OPD and OPD with task-level RLVR-adapted teachers. These results highlight the importance of adapting teachers not only to solve problems independently, but also to provide reliable guidance on the states their students actually visit.

Reasoning language models write a chain of thought before they answer, and many checks in it do not change the final answer. Natural language autoencoders (NLAs) explain a model's activations in text and rebuild activations from text, and editing an explanation can steer a model at a single token. We study whether such edits can also steer how a model reasons. We build VERS (Verbalize, Edit, Reconstruct, Steer): it turns explanation edits at the doubt points of one reasoning trace into one fixed direction, and adds this direction at paragraph breaks. On the full GSM8K test, VERS cuts generated tokens by 45.8% at an accuracy change of -0.82 percentage points (pp); on MATH-500 it saves 31.3% at -3.05 pp. At half strength, its trade-off between tokens and accuracy lies between those of the published methods SEAL and DEER. On competition problems, however, VERS loses 3-7 pp at every model size, and a fixed direction did not separate necessary checks from unneeded ones.

规范感知的预测不确定性分解

3/10
Specification-Aware Predictive Uncertainty Decomposition
Roman Vashurin, Mikhail Seleznyov, Assel Yermekova, Fedor Chernogorskii, Danila ...
个性化推荐理由:

该论文研究预测不确定性分解，与LLM可信度相关，但未直接涉及推荐、搜索或广告中的排序建模或Transformer架构改进。其潜在应用价值在于通过不确定性估计提升推荐系统对噪声特征的鲁棒性，但相关性较弱。

2026-11-02 11:23:22 | arXiv:2611.02956v1 |
stat.MLcs.AIcs.CLcs.LG
查看完整摘要
Classical decompositions of predictive uncertainty, such as the information-theoretic one, are defined for a fixed representation $x$ of the input object. They do not consider that $x$ may be incomplete, nor do they measure how much the missing information can affect the prediction. In Large Language Models, users can clarify an underspecified input without any retraining. The clarified input can describe the user's unobserved intent $z^*$ more accurately and change the predictive uncertainty about the outcome. In this paper, we formalize this setting probabilistically and derive a three-term decomposition of prediction risk into specification, residual aleatoric, and epistemic uncertainties. We show that at the original information level, when only $x$ is available, the aleatoric uncertainty, represented by a Bayes risk, has two components. The first, which we call specification uncertainty, is the expected reduction in this Bayes risk from revealing the intent. The second is the residual aleatoric uncertainty, i.e., the Bayes risk that remains once the intent is known, averaged over its possible values $z$ given $x$. The distinction is important, as specification uncertainty informs the decision to request clarification before making a prediction. We build approximations of these components and connect them to existing uncertainty measures. Our experiments across different domains and modalities show distinct roles for the three components. Specification uncertainty is the most informative for detecting underspecified inputs, while residual aleatoric uncertainty is the most informative for detecting in-distribution prediction errors.

查看完整摘要
Language model agents increasingly act on users' behalf by manipulating external environments through tools, often from high-level instructions that leave critical task- and environment-specific context unstated. To prevent resulting misaligned actions, recent work verifies proposed actions before execution, largely by establishing whether the conditions supporting them hold. We argue that this confirmation-oriented view leaves a fundamental blind spot: a wrong action often passes every check that asks whether it is right. This motivates a different question for verification: rather than asking what evidence supports an action, can the agent find evidence that refutes it? We pursue this direction with BlackSwan, a refutation-oriented, expect-suspect-test procedure that predicts the intended effect of each state-mutating action, formulates specific and empirically testable hypotheses for how it could be wrong, and tests them using existing observations or additional non-mutating actions, executing the action only if none survives testing. On challenging AppWorld tasks, BlackSwan improves success rate over the strongest baseline by 14.9 and 25.2%p with and without the backbone model's thinking mode, respectively. Even without thinking or in-context demonstrations, it outperforms stronger baselines equipped with them. The gains also transfer beyond AppWorld, with BlackSwan improving WebShop performance by 12.7%p. More broadly, our results suggest that language model agents have an underused capacity to recognize and self-correct misaligned actions, which can be systematically elicited through active conjecture and refutation before execution.

A State Change Is Local, Its Consequences Are Not: Dependency-Aware State Revision for Long-Term Agent Memory

cs.CL
查看完整摘要
Long-lived agents rely on persistent memory of users, tasks and decisions, and this memory must stay consistent with a world that keeps changing. When an upstream fact changes, however, memory systems can revise the contradicted memory itself while the beliefs, plans and actions derived from it remain accepted, even though they have lost their support. We therefore formulate dependency-aware state revision, which asks not only what changed but also which dependent states should no longer be trusted. To address it, StateGraph maintains an evolving state dependency graph whose edges are recovered from interaction histories. Its core design is counterfactual verification: each candidate edge is tested by asking whether the dependent state would survive the loss of its premise, which separates support dependence from semantic relatedness, and revision then invalidates a dependent state only when a strict dependency fails and no independent support path survives. To test this design, we introduce StateChangeBench, a controlled benchmark of 50 author-constructed cases annotated with cascade depth, hard negatives and action consequences. On it, StateGraph's Impact-F1 margin over a CUPMem reimplementation is 6.9 percentage points at depth 1 and 26.0 at depth 3+. On public long-term memory benchmarks, its point estimates are comparable to or higher than those of the evaluated baselines on most datasets. These results provide initial evidence that explicit dependency tracking can improve selective memory revision under changing conditions.

When a masked diffusion language model is sampled in a few parallel steps in a random order, the model actually run is a mixture over reveal plans, while cross-entropy training fits token conditionals rather than this mixture. We introduce mixture-likelihood fine-tuning (Mix-FT), which fine-tunes a pretrained model on an importance-weighted bound of its own sampler's likelihood, with no teacher and no change to the architecture or the sampler. 查看完整摘要
Steering vectors align a model at inference time without touching its weights, but eliciting several personas at once is a different problem from eliciting one. Prior multi-persona steering approaches predetermine both how persona vectors are combined and the specific layers at which they are injected. Moreover, this static commitment introduces substantial offline search costs to identify suitable target layers, despite the fact that optimal intervention strengths vary dynamically across both decoding steps and distinct personas. We introduce SYNC (SensitivitY-guided iNference Control), a novel training-free inference control framework compatible with pre-existing steering vectors. SYNC dynamically estimates each layer's sensitivity to every persona via cosine similarity trajectories, assigning each persona to its optimal injection layer at every decoding step. Across two models, two persona profiles and steering vectors from both unsupervised and supervised extraction, SYNC leads on 21 of 28 persona configurations, significantly on 15. Code is available at \href{https://github.com/yosefnuragaw/sync}{this https URL}.

Scalaris：面向对话式AI助手的可扩展企业内存架构

3/10
Scalaris: A Scalable Enterprise Memory Architecture for Conversational AI Assistants
Devarsh Gandhi, Sarthak Pawar, Hemprasad Badgujar, Vaibhav Rathore, Siddharth Su...
个性化推荐理由:

该论文聚焦对话式AI助手的企业级记忆架构，属于LLM智能体记忆管理的系统基础设施，与推荐/搜索/广告排序无直接关联。其可扩展记忆存储与检索机制或可类比用于用户历史行为序列的长程记忆建模，但标题未体现任何RecSys/Search/Ads应用场景，相关性较弱。

2026-11-02 06:37:11 | arXiv:2611.02612v1 |
cs.CLcs.AI
查看完整摘要
Enterprise conversational systems require memory to personalize interactions, retain information across sessions, and maintain conversational coherence. At enterprise scale, however, memory must not only retrieve relevant information accurately but also provide it efficiently to the answer-generation model. Existing memory systems face a trade-off between retrieval accuracy and context efficiency. Retrieving more context can improve recall but increases inference cost and latency. We present Scalaris, a scalable enterprise memory architecture that addresses this trade-off through a unified long-term and short-term memory design. We propose that user interactions and assistant responses possess distinct characteristics and roles in a conversation: user interactions capture evolving knowledge about the user and provide value across sessions, whereas assistant responses must be stored efficiently so that only information relevant to the current query is retrieved. We therefore treat them separately and support each with specialized representations and ingestion/retrieval pipelines. Scalaris reaches 92.2 % accuracy on LongMemEval-S and 90.77 % on LoCoMo using Qwen3-32B, state-of-the-art results on both benchmarks, while supplying the answer-generation model with approximately 10 times fewer retrieved context tokens than Hindsight, a leading memory system for conversational AI. When integrated with our proprietary closed-source AI assistants, Scalaris improved overall response quality by 36 %. Together, these results establish Scalaris as a cost-effective framework for building scalable, personalized conversational AI systems.

Chain on Demand: Deferred Evidence Organization for Long-Term Conversational Memory

2026-11-02 03:54:52 | arXiv:2611.02480v1 |
cs.CLcs.AI
查看完整摘要
Long-term conversational memory faces a timing mismatch: write-time summaries and graphs organize history before a future query is known, while independent retrieval can allocate a limited evidence budget to redundant or fragmented turns. We introduce Chain on Demand (CoD), a query-time evidence organization framework over an unchanged turn-level memory. Given a broad candidate pool, CoD constructs a temporary evidence chain by allocating a fixed anchor budget according to query relevance, lexical diversity relative to selected anchors, and newly covered query terms, followed by deterministic restoration of each selected anchor's same-session neighborhood. The resulting chain is used only for the current query, while the underlying conversational memory remains unchanged. In a paired comparison over all 1,986 LoCoMo questions, CoD achieves 82.2% accuracy on 1,540 non-adversarial questions and 76.3% on all 1,986 questions. On LongMemEval-S, one fixed configuration that indexes both user and assistant turns obtains 86.6% across all 500 questions. These results show that long-term memory can preserve the original conversation while deferring evidence organization until the information need is known.

如预期？通过期望-观测一致性验证实现高效LLM规划

3/10
As Expected? Efficient LLM Planning via Expectation-Observation Consistency Verification
Qingfu Zhu, Huanyang Zheng, Ting Liu, Wanxiang Che
个性化推荐理由:

该论文聚焦LLM规划效率，通过期望-观测一致性验证提升规划质量，属于LLM智能体/规划领域的通用技术，与推荐、搜索、广告排序无直接关联。其一致性验证思想理论上可类比用于推荐中的序列决策或对话式推荐规划，但论文本身未涉及该场景，相关性较低。

2026-11-02 02:59:57 | arXiv:2611.02438v1 |
cs.CL
查看完整摘要
LLM planning often relies on reactive executors (e.g., ReAct) that generate a single action at each step to remain grounded in the latest observations, incurring substantial inference cost. Batched executors reduce this cost by predicting multiple actions in a single model invocation, yet they are prone to compounding errors, as intermediate observations are ignored. We propose an expectation-observation consistency verification (EOCV) mechanism for batched executors, preserving grounding while improving efficiency. The mechanism generates expectations of action outcomes and compares them with the resulting observations. Consistency permits continued execution, whereas mismatches trigger early termination and replanning from the current observation. Experiments on text-based and multimodal benchmarks across multiple backbones show broadly comparable average task performance between EOCV and reactive executors, with a 29-35% reduction in average weighted token usage. Moreover, the mechanism is model-agnostic and remains effective when integrated into state-of-the-art planners.

cs.SDcs.CLeess.AS
查看完整摘要
Most benchmarks for real-time voice systems focus on one aspect, such as turn-taking, instruction following, paralinguistic control, or conversational memory, each with its own data and set of systems. We present InteractionBench, a benchmark and evaluation harness that measures four dimensions on the same systems from the same recorded user audio: conversational timing, spoken-task accuracy, whisper and volume, and groundedness in conversations of more than twenty turns. The benchmark contains 221 items per system in thirteen scenarios. Before any system runs, we fix the expected floor-control actions for spoken tasks by rule and take the expected answers from scripted dialogues. We score whispering from the audio signal. We evaluate GPT-Live, GPT-Realtime, Gemini Live, Moshi, PersonaPlex, Freeze-Omni, and a turn-based cascade in 1,519 graded runs. Among the six full-duplex systems, timing and spoken-task accuracy are rank-correlated ($ρ= 0.83$), while the cascade has the highest spoken-task score (38/50) and the second-lowest timing score. No system corrects a user's grammar within the required window. Only Gemini Live whispers on most requests (8/10). Hosted systems pass 67-88% of groundedness probes, compared with 14-35% for open full-duplex models. Timing is measured where the client hears each system, so the timing of hosted systems includes network delay: for GPT-Live, whose server reports its own clock, removing a delay of about 1.5 s lowers its timing score from 86% to 56%. We also document where the protocol differs across systems, including the text instructions.

大语言模型作为形式化器时的求解器式行为

3/10
Solver-Like Behavior in Large Language Models as Formalizers
Rikhil Amonkar, Stuti Mohan, Kevin Nguyen, Ceyhun Efe Kayan, Ronan Le Bras, Li Z...
个性化推荐理由:

该论文探讨LLM在形式化任务中表现出类似求解器的行为，属于LLM推理与形式化能力的学术研究，未涉及RecSys、Search或Ads的应用场景或技术。虽然LLM能力提升可能间接影响推荐系统，但本文焦点与我的核心方向关联较弱。

2026-11-02 02:12:55 | arXiv:2611.02395v1 |
cs.CLcs.AI
查看完整摘要
In the LLM-as-formalizer paradigm, a large language model translates a problem into a formal program and leaves the search to a solver, gaining the solver's guarantees. Formalizers are now often reasoning models that write a long reasoning trace before the program, yet they are judged only by the program's output. We find that the trace routinely does the solver's job, assigning, testing and ruling out values until it often reaches the full answer; we call this solver-like behavior (SLB). We define an ontology of SLB over the trace, the program and its output, and use LLM judges, checked against human labels, to label 2,000 generations from three reasoning models on four constraint satisfaction datasets, each formalized in a declarative and an imperative formal language. SLB appears in 85% of traces and takes 24% of reasoning tokens, yet the program usually recomputes the answer, so the effort is redundant. The 9% of programs that inherit the trace's answer also inherit its errors, and pass output-based evaluation whenever that answer is correct. The amount of SLB is not associated with accuracy, and SLB is more common in imperative Python than in declarative Z3 or PDDL. Cautionary prompts reduce it by only a quarter, but hiding the problem's values behind placeholders, which the program receives only at run time, removes 97% of it without loss of accuracy. Formalizers should therefore be evaluated by how their programs are reached, not only by what they output.

MemShift：将LLM衍生的个人记忆中的检测与修复分离

3/10
MemShift: Separating Detection from Repair in LLM-Derived Personal Memory
Zichuan Xu, Haochen Zhang, Yuyang Dong, Makoto Onizuka, Chuan Xiao
个性化推荐理由:

该论文针对LLM驱动的个人记忆系统，将记忆错误的检测与修复解耦，属于个人化记忆管理方向，与推荐/搜索/广告的核心排序或召回问题关联较弱。其潜在价值在于用户画像/长期记忆纠错可间接提升个性化推荐中的用户建模质量，但论文本身未聚焦RecSys/Search/Ads场景，因此相关性较低。

2026-11-02 00:35:19 | arXiv:2611.02338v1 |
cs.CLcs.AIcs.LG
查看完整摘要
AI assistants store values that a language model derived from a person's records under the person's own definitions, such as their important contacts. Keeping such a value correct takes two steps: detection, noticing that an input changed, and repair, producing the correct new value. Benchmarks that score only the final answer cannot tell which step failed, and they state every change as new text. Seeing this limitation, we present MemShift, a benchmark of five shifts: source revision, deletion, redefinition, writer switch, and export. The first three fork a synthetic world into branches that differ only in the shift. Gold values and dependencies are fixed before any text, and a deterministic scorer replaces an LLM judge. For a memory that reports its invalidations, MemShift shows for each changed item whether the memory noticed the change and whether its repair was right. Moreover, we build a reference memory with known detection and repair to validate the measure. By evaluating four open-weight LLMs, we find that detection helps only as much as repair allows: the LLMs re-derive few values computed by the person's definitions correctly, and programs that the LLM writes over typed records close most of the gap to exact recomputation. Code and data are available at https://github.com/zichuanxu/memshift.

自适应停止何时奏效？循环语言模型中的选择规则至关重要

2026-11-02 00:28:21 | arXiv:2611.02333v1 |
cs.LGcs.CL
查看完整摘要
Looped language models can stop iterating early, and recent work shows that post-hoc readouts of the loop trajectory often rival the learned exit gate of the looped model Ouro. Such comparisons reduce each method to one operating point chosen by a selection rule. We ask whether that rule decides the outcome. On a frozen Ouro-1.4B with four loops, we compared a trained stability probe ($π_1$-x) with Ouro's native gate, adapted to a per-question stopping decision, in pre-registered studies; the confirmatory study was designed after an exploratory finding on development data and evaluated once on a sealed test set of 8,517 questions. In the pre-registered confirmatory comparison, which selected operating points with a bootstrap-screened 1 pp accuracy-loss rule, the probe saved more of the four-loop budget than the native gate (+0.053 gross macro saving over three benchmarks, 95% CI [+0.049, +0.057]), mostly from CommonsenseQA, where the probe's test upper bound on accuracy loss exceeded 1 pp. Under a point-only rule, a pre-specified secondary analysis on the same test set reversed the preference (-0.022 [-0.026, -0.018]), with operating points that exceeded the budget. In pre-specified secondary comparisons, the probe showed no demonstrated advantage over an untrained KL divergence between successive loops (+0.00125 [-0.0041, +0.0065]); we emphasise this result post hoc and claim no equivalence. The probe also broke its budget on one of two out-of-distribution benchmarks (3.26 pp loss on Social IQa). Savings are estimated from stored full-depth traces, not measured inference time, and our screen carries no formal risk-control guarantee. We conclude that halting methods should be compared as signals together with selection procedures, with strong untrained baselines and out-of-distribution checks.

通过模块化两阶段开放集识别规避幅度坍塌

3/10
Circumventing Magnitude Collapse via Modular Two-Stage Open-Set Recognition
Laurin van den Bergh, Halil Bisgin, Andres Palechor, Mike Suter, Manuel Günther
个性化推荐理由:

该论文聚焦开放集识别（Open-Set Recognition），属于机器学习/视觉领域的通用问题，与推荐、搜索、广告的核心排序或建模关联较弱。文中针对的'幅度坍塌'（magnitude collapse）与OOD/开放集问题可能间接启发推荐系统中的冷启动或未见物品处理，但缺乏明确的RecSys/Search/Ads场景指向。整体相关性偏低。

2026-11-02 16:44:08 | arXiv:2611.03421v1 |
cs.CV
查看完整摘要
Open-set recognition (OSR) requires neural networks to classify known classes while rejecting unknown samples, which is critical for real-world deployment. So far, OSR research studied and developed representation learning and postprocessing methods independently and their interactions remain unexplored. In this paper, we reconsider OSR as a modular two-stage pipeline and present the first systematic study of interactions between representation learning and postprocessing methods across dataset scales and usage of known unknown data. First, we discover a failure mode we term \emph{magnitude collapse}, where representation learning methods that utilize known unknown data can suffer performance degradation at large scale and irreversibly destroy discriminative information, despite excelling at small scale. Second, we study the modular interactions and reveal when they can be leveraged for modular performance gains via two-stage processing and where interaction effects amplify performance degradation due to magnitude collapse. Third, we show how these findings can be used to achieve state-of-the-art performance with a simple $K+1$ class classifier baseline and magnitude-aware postprocessing while circumventing magnitude collapse. Finally, our results demonstrate that small-scale evaluations with known unknown data are not predictive of large-scale performance, invalidating current best practices in OSR research. Code available at https://github.com/TwoDigitsOneNumber/Two-Stage-OSR.

While Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards (RLVR) has significantly advanced multimodal reasoning, existing frameworks suffer from a critical "perception-reasoning gap." Due to an over-reliance on seemingly relevant visual tokens, even minor perceptual perturbations can propagate through the reasoning process, leading to compounding hallucinations. To address this issue, we propose MIRA, a training framework that improves robustness to erroneous visual contexts while enhancing logical reasoning ability. By injecting filtered, deceptive visual contexts during training, we construct challenging "hard traps" that stress-test the model's robustness to misleading information. Furthermore, we introduce a group-wise reflection-triggering mechanism that enables the model to autonomously detect and rectify inconsistencies between visual cues and logical constraints, which is ultimately internalized as an intrinsic policy behavior. Importantly, MIRA does not rely on human annotations. Experimental results across multiple reasoning and perception benchmarks show that MIRA yields an average 7% improvement on in-domain benchmarks, with consistent improvements on vision-centric benchmarks. These findings highlight that active error recovery is essential for robust multimodal reasoning. Comprehensive ablation studies and analyses further provide insights into why MIRA is effective.

VL-Dedup: Graded Multimodal Entity Resolution for Image-Text Data Deduplication
Zhenxi Liu, Guanze Chen, Dehui Yu, Xingyu Wang, Bingxu Hou, Chengwei Li, Tao Ma,...
个性化推荐理由:

该论文聚焦于图文数据的多模态实体解析与去重，属于数据清洗/数据质量管理范畴，并非推荐、搜索或广告排序的核心建模技术。其多模态实体匹配思想或可迁移至异构数据（如用户序列与上下文特征）的统一建模，但与VLM类比用于统一推荐建模的关注点差异较大，故相关性较低。

2026-11-02 13:06:20 | arXiv:2611.03080v1 |
cs.CV
查看完整摘要
Image-text corpora contain copies of one image, captions that describe one image in different ways, captions reused for different images, and captions that do not match their images. Each case calls for a different curation action. Deduplication and multimodal entity resolution (ER) only decide whether records are the same, and they are evaluated on labeled pairs. Yet pair scores do not tell which records end up in the same cluster or which useful captions get deleted. We therefore formulate image-text deduplication as graded multimodal ER, which decides visual identity, caption handling, and mismatches for each pair, and we evaluate it at three levels: pairs, clusters, and the retained set. Our method, VL-Dedup, groups records of the same visual entity into clusters and caps the size of each cluster. Within each cluster, a vision-language model (VLM) and CLIPScore decide which captions to keep and which to flag. We also build VL-DedupBench, with 50,000 COCO and SOP records and 66,788 labeled pairs. On it, a classifier that selects no false edge among the labeled pairs still joins millions of record pairs from different entities. Spending VLM calls on the pairs inside clusters, rather than on pairs with uncertain scores, gives better caption decisions from the same model. Even when almost all clusters are correct, keeping one record per cluster loses facts in many deletions, while the deletions of VL-Dedup lose much less information.

视觉语言模型作为行人重识别的评判者

VLM-as-a-Judge for Person Re-Identification
Bessie Dominguez-Dager, Miguel Cazorla, Vincent Lepetit

2026-11-02 12:22:19 | arXiv:2611.03027v1 |
cs.CV
查看完整摘要
Cloth-changing person re-identification (CC-ReID) is still very challenging, and no method clearly dominates: methods that lead on one benchmark collapse on the others, because they rest on different identity cues. We show that a general-purpose vision-language model (VLM), used zero-shot as a judge for reranking a short candidate list, beats the expert retrieval models it reranks by a wide and consistent margin. On the four scenarios of CHIRLA, a long-term CC-ReID benchmark that no publicly released Re-ID model is trained on, reranking the candidates of two frozen experts raises rank-1 on every scenario, by 12.9 points on average over the best expert and 9.4 over the best naive ensemble of the two, with no component fine-tuned on the target data. Merging both experts' shortlists matches or beats reranking the stronger expert's shortlist alone, at lower cost. Introducing a VLM does, however, come at a cost: almost all of the pipeline's FLOPs are spent on it. We therefore borrow a criterion from ensembling and invoke the VLM judge only where the two experts disagree at rank 1. This threshold-free gate removes 40% of the calls, and a comparable share of total compute, with no significant drop in rank-1, trading 1.8 to 3.8 points of mAP. Judging remains the dominant cost, but on a benchmark where dedicated experts stall, we find a frozen general-purpose model to be the strongest judge of identity. The code is available at https://github.com/bdager/JudgeReID.

RouteREPA：面向纯图像Transformer的路由表示对齐

3/10
RouteREPA: Routed Representation Alignment for Just Image Transformers
Yi Tan, Beier Zhu, Sirui Chen, Cheng Chen, Xingyu Zhu, Yuan Zhou, Zhenshan Tan, ...
个性化推荐理由:

该论文聚焦于纯图像Transformer的表征对齐与路由机制，属于视觉架构层面的改进，与推荐系统、搜索或广告的直接相关性较弱。虽然其中涉及的路由/表示对齐思想在理论上可能迁移到多模态推荐中的特征对齐，但标题未体现任何RecSys/Search/Ads场景或异构数据建模动机，因此得分较低。

2026-11-02 11:09:03 | arXiv:2611.02941v1 |
cs.CVcs.LG
查看完整摘要
Representation alignment (REPA) is widely used to accelerate the training of latent-space diffusion transformers (DiTs). However, applying it to pixel-space models such as Just Image Transformers (JiT) is challenging. Our analysis reveals a pronounced representation conflict between discriminative alignment and pixel-space generation. Compared with its latent counterpart, JiT learns representations that are substantially less aligned with DINOv2. Applying REPA to JiT consistently reduces the reconstructability of its intermediate representations, whereas this effect is not observed in Latent JiT. We attribute this conflict to JiT directly reconstructing pixels, which requires its representations to hierarchically encode discriminative semantics and reconstruction-relevant visual information across network depths. To address these challenges, we propose Routed Representation Alignment (RouteREPA), which uses lightweight, representation-dependent routers to adaptively route task-oriented representations for alignment and generation. We further apply routing across multiple network depths to accommodate their depth-dependent representation requirements. Extensive experiments on ImageNet-1K at 256x256 resolution show that RouteREPA consistently improves JiT across model scales and training budgets, outperforming standard REPA. On JiT-H/16, RouteREPA further surpasses PixelREPA using only one-third of its training epochs without classifier-free guidance.

Unlocking the Potential of Vision Foundation Models for Multi-Task Dense Prediction via Mixture of Task Adapters

2026-11-02 08:47:07 | arXiv:2611.02769v1 |
cs.CV
查看完整摘要
Multi-task dense prediction seeks a single, coherent representation that supports simultaneously discriminative semantic recognition and regularized geometric/structural inference. For vision foundation models, pretrained semantic invariances conflict with the frequency and smoothness biases of dense objectives; joint fine-tuning and single-route adapters therefore compress competing gradients into one update channel, causing negative transfer and unstable cross-task agreement. We frame this as two-level adaptation: task-generic alignment that preserves priors, and task-aware specialization that resolves late-stage conflicts. Based on this principle, we propose D2MoE , a Mixture of Task Adapters that explicitly factorizes these roles within a frozen DINOv3 backbone. D2MoE introduces Task-Generic Low-Rank Adaptation (TG-LoRA) in attention projections throughout the network to provide a stable, shared adaptation channel, and a Task-Adaptive Mixture of Experts (TA-MoE) injected only at the final attention block of each stage to enact sparse, task-conditioned perturbations of token mixing in a single forward pass. A Semantic Prior Injector further couples multi-level task features with frozen foundation priors to reinforce semantic coherence while preserving boundary and structural fidelity. Experiments across diverse benchmarks substantiate that this structured factorization yields robust multi-task improvements with a small trainable footprint, establishing a principled and scalable route to prior-preserving, foundation-centric multi-task dense perception.

Ying Dai

2026-11-02 07:37:38 | arXiv:2611.02686v1 |
cs.CV
查看完整摘要
Semantic granularity, which refers to the levels at which visual representations organize image content into meaningful entities, is fundamental to image understanding. However, whether pretrained visual representations inherently encode semantic structures at multiple granularities remains poorly understood. This paper presents a representation-driven approach for discovering semantically meaningful image regions at different granularities through clustering of pretrained visual representations without semantic supervision. Specifically, we concatenate selected feature maps extracted from a pretrained visual model and apply a singular value decomposition (SVD)-based projection to derive pixel-wise principal representation maps (PRMs). The resulting PRMs capture implicit semantic structures embedded in pretrained visual representations, making them accessible to subsequent semantic clustering. We then introduce the semantic continuity rate (SCR), based on the silhouette principle, to quantify the coherence of pixel-wise representations within and between clusters at different candidate granularities. Furthermore, we analyze the signal-to-noise ratio (SNR) of SCR profiles to assess representation suitability for discovering semantic regions at multiple granularities. Our results indicate that PRMs exhibiting higher SNR values over an initial range of cluster numbers tend to yield more semantically meaningful regions across multiple granularities. We evaluate the proposed approach on the COCO-2017 and VOC-2012 validation datasets using Hungarian-matched intersection over union (HIoU). The results demonstrate competitive segmentation performance among the compared methods, supporting the feasibility of discovering useful semantic granularities through clustering PRMs without semantic supervision.
