Artificial intelligence (AI) technologies and their capabilities have advanced rapidly over the past few years, leading to widespread interest and adoption in the biological and ecological sciences. However, the data infrastructure and computational resources that provide the foundation for AI workflows in ecology and biodiversity science remain fragmented. Moreover, the flow of value remains largely unidirectional: data move from collectors, through repositories and aggregators into models, and finally to research products or management actions. The derived value, such as corrected taxonomies, annotations, quality flags, and insights, rarely returns to source communities. We propose an extension of the original FAIR principles to address the needs of AI-ready infrastructure: the AI-FAIR metadata criteria, which place AI-enabled science in the context of a bidirectional Question-Data-Model triangle. This framework is designed to facilitate large-scale content-based analysis across diverse collections and ensure that the scientists generating the data benefit from the AI-enabled advances built on their data. Alongside these criteria, we introduce an agentic evaluation workflow that both rates existing datasets on the AI-FAIR and traditional FAIR criteria and provides actionable feedback where critical metadata is missing or incomplete. This modular dataset evaluation tool, paired with a domain-appropriate checklist, translates these principles into a reusable assessment of machine-learning readiness, AI-readiness for specified tasks, traceability, and ethical and CARE-aligned stewardship. Through this approach we aim to create a community-informed system to close the model data loop, so that AI also enriches data to further enable scientific discovery.

Dhārā Bench is a retrieval benchmark for Indian Knowledge Systems (IKS), with 325 questions in 12 categories and 60,121 text chunks from 15 source groups. The categories were developed based on 4,500 questions submitted to the Dhārā application; the evaluation set combines corpus-aligned application queries with expert-contributed questions. The annotations comprise 7,578 question-chunk relevance links involving 6,847 distinct corpus chunks. For 34 composite and multi-hop questions, 401 conjugate groups specify alternative sets of passages that jointly support each question. Chunk identifiers and hierarchical references connect the judgments to their source texts. The paper describes corpus preparation, annotation, and validation, and reports a fixed BM25 baseline implemented with Pyserini with $k_1=0.9$ and $b=0.4$. Across all questions, BM25 achieves MRR@20 of 0.315, Recall@10 of 0.182, and nDCG@10 of 0.205. On the group-annotated subset, complete-evidence scores are CE_Recall@20 = 0.294 and CE_MRR@20 = 0.141. The category results expose particularly weak verse retrieval, while group scores reveal gaps between finding individual passages and completing their support. The corpus and evaluation code are available at https://github.com/Avinash2236/Dhara-Bench.

Remote sensing image fusion and semantic interpretation are usually optimized separately, so reconstruction quality need not preserve downstream retrieval utility. We propose LANCE, which uses structured language as an explicit, indexable semantic state. A frozen vision-language model maps the input to a closed-vocabulary text record; a language-conditioned generator fuses the imagery; and a dense verifier with a differentiable continuous summarizer re-describes the output. Text-text consistency and anti-collapse contrastive text-mask losses with negative queries close the loop without dense annotations or VLM fine-tuning. Text perturbations, cross-verifier transfer, and pseudo-RGB ablations examine the mechanisms underlying these gains. The framework connects image fusion to verifiable semantic representations for multimodal retrieval and knowledge management.

GUI web agents require realistic and interactive environments that remain stable during both evaluation and training. Live website evaluation suffers from changing content, access restrictions, and limited coverage of authenticated workflows, while existing synthetic environments for training often lack functional depth and visual fidelity. We propose WebHarbor, a suite of 153 executable and dockerized website environments that faithfully reconstruct the visual interfaces, content, and functional workflows of real-world web services under deterministic control. We build WebHarbor through task-driven human-agent collaboration, where environments evolve as new tasks expand their functional scope, coding agents implement the required changes, and human contributors audit visual fidelity, functional correctness, and task validity. D&A: Decoupling Description and Assessment Enables VLMs as Zero-Shot Robotic Reward Models
Marius Memmel, Shawn Yin, Urvi Rutia, Angad Wadhwa, Eldon Li, Gavin Angeloff, An...

cs.ROcs.AIcs.CLcs.LGeess.SY
查看完整摘要
Vision-Language Models (VLMs) hold promise for zero-shot video task-progress estimation in robot learning, yet existing frameworks frequently suffer from confirmation bias, hallucinating progress in failed executions due to implicit success assumptions or relative temporal ranking designs. Naive prompting of frontier models collapses into a binary success detector that fails to capture nuanced, non-linear progress and failures. While post-training approaches can mitigate this through explicit training, they demand expensive real-world robotics data and require computationally intensive post-training for every individual base model. To resolve these limitations, we introduce D&A (Describe & Assess): our approach decouples progress estimation into an objective, task-grounded textual description of scene dynamics followed by an assessment phase using explicit rubrics. By compelling the VLM to synthesize an explicit, text-based definition of absolute success before scoring, the framework anchors evaluations to an external, global criterion rather than the video's internal timeline. Empirical results demonstrate that our method calibrates zero-shot estimates and mitigates confirmation bias, providing fine-grained, partial progress metrics that accurately penalize failed executions while correctly interpolating successful runs. Being purely prompt-based, D&A generalizes across tasks, embodiments, and benchmarks---matching or surpassing post-trained reward models---and its reward estimates enable efficient downstream reinforcement learning.

2026-11-02 16:45:03 | arXiv:2611.03424v1 |
cs.CL
查看完整摘要
Research taste, the judgment of what to read, which problems to pursue, and which methods to try, increasingly shapes the work of AI scientists, yet it remains far less understood than their ability to execute research. We introduce IdeaTaste, a benchmark that scores an LLM by how closely the research proposals it writes from a literature snapshot frozen at 2025 match the work the same communities published in 2026. Our experiments span six contemporary language models and four research areas from established to emerging fields, covering more than 90{,}000 proposals in total. LLM agents fall well short of human communities in both the literature they cite and the ideas they state, reaching about 26\% of the human level on citation matching and only 18\% on idea matching. Their populations can cite as diversely as a community and take up its tasks and methods, yet rarely arrive at the concrete approaches and research plans it pursues. Neither richer field context nor larger populations substantially close this gap. In blinded expert review, AI proposals trail the 2026 papers least in presentation and most in expected success, echoing their gap in concrete approaches, and those whose ideas no human paper shares fare worst, suggesting weaker directions rather than overlooked opportunities. These results indicate that the main bottleneck in AI research taste lies less in finding the directions a community works on than in turning them into the concrete approaches it goes on to pursue. We release IdeaTaste as an open, continuously updated leaderboard (https://cxcscmu.github.io/IdeaTaste) that benchmarks contemporary AI proposals against future publication cycles.

2026-11-02 15:44:02 | arXiv:2611.03317v1 |
cs.CL
查看完整摘要
Activation steering offers a lightweight mechanism for controlling model behavior, but existing methods depend on sentence-level contrastive data that is unavailable for most of the world's languages. We show that multilingual sentiment lexicons are a viable substitute across three model families: aggregated multilingual lexicons outperform English-derived steering vectors for mid- and low-resource languages, while monolingual lexicons remain stronger for languages a model has specialized in during pre-training. The steering efficacy is mechanistically dictated by both representational density and pre-training priors. Large-magnitude English vectors overwhelm the compact representations of low-resource languages, causing "linguistic collapse." This fragility is mitigated by the norm-dampening of multilingual aggregation. Architectural biases, including bilingual specialization penalty and latent numerical rigidity, also heavily constrain cross-lingual steering responsiveness. Ultimately, our work establishes lexicons as a practical steering signal and offers a comprehensive mechanistic account of cross-lingual steering dynamics.

Previous research shows that vision-language (VL) models struggle to understand fine-grained image details and tend to focus on the overall layout. Moreover, any weakness in English tends to be further amplified in the multilingual setting. To study that, we extend InpaintCOCO, an English dataset focused on fine-grained visual details, to 13 typologically diverse languages across five language families, creating MultiInpaintCOCO - a new multilingual benchmark of 953 minimal-pair examples. We further test several state-of-the-art models in multilingual multimodal retrieval, considering both light VL CLIP-like encoders and large language models. Our results show that performance degrades substantially for lower-resource languages, with the strongest predictor being the data resources available for the languages rather than typological similarity to English. This bias varies considerably across architectures. Among the tested models, Gemma 3 shows the strongest English preference while Qwen 3 is the least biased, despite both reaching similar monolingual accuracy across languages. cs.CRcs.AIcs.CL
查看完整摘要
LLM agents compact long histories to fit their context windows, but the compacted context must still carry the operating rules that govern later actions. Prior work shows that violations rise after compaction, but measures compliance outside a complete agent harness and does not identify where each violation comes from. We introduce ARCBench(Agent Rule Compliance Benchmark), a configurable benchmark built on the open-source pi agent harness. Its tool-calling agent loop lets us measure rule compliance from actions taken during execution, bringing the evaluation closer to deployed agent workflows. Using ARCBench, we examine where violations after compaction come from, and how different compaction configurations and adversarial settings change their sources. We find that compaction rarely deletes the rule: most violations after compaction occur while the rule is still in the compacted history, with the agent treating a later request as permission, ignoring the rule, and proceeding anyway. Where violations come from depends on the configuration: how the rule is delivered decides whether the agent would break it even without compaction, while the compaction strategy and budget decide whether the rule survives. The strongest compactor-targeted attack more than doubles violations by corrupting the rule in the summary and changing how the agents behave, whereas the strongest defense restores the rule in most summary but still violates. Finally, retaining a rule does not guarantee compliance: compacted runs that retain the rule violate more than twice as often as runs with the full history.

2026-11-02 14:18:04 | arXiv:2611.03184v1 |
cs.CL
查看完整摘要
Large language agents for literary role-playing are typically modeled around dialogue generation, leaving characters' non-dialogue behaviors and their dynamic evolution over story time underrepresented. In this paper, we propose ACTOR (Action-Centered Temporal Organization for Literary Role-Playing Agents), an automated framework for constructing such behavior trajectories from raw novel texts. ACTOR extracts dialogue and persona-relevant non-dialogue behaviors, represents each behavior using a Thought-Action-Observation structure, and organizes behavior trajectories according to the internal temporal order of the story. We build a behavior dataset from 143 diverse literary works. For each source behavior, we generate counterfactual behaviors that are contextually plausible yet inconsistent with the character's persona. We find that existing models struggle to reliably infer character-consistent actions from historical behaviors and evolving character states. Using these behavior pairs, we train models with Regularized Preference Optimization (RPO), which augments DPO with a negative log-likelihood loss on the source behavior to jointly optimize behavior preference and generation. We further design a four-way next-behavior selection task to evaluate the model's ability to faithfully mimic character actions. Across 17,560 evaluation instances, the model trained on ACTOR's preference pairs achieves substantially improved accuracy. This result demonstrates that structured, temporally organized supervision enables models to leverage character profiles and prior behaviors to infer subsequent actions, greatly advancing literary role-playing beyond mere dialogue imitation. Code and data are available at https://github.com/SilentStar233/ACTOR.

On July 1, 2025, Vietnam reduced its 63 provinces to 34, abolished its 696 districts, and replaced its 10,035 commune-level units (wards) with 3,321. ViAdminShift, a Vietnamese language resource about this event, consists of 7,995 benchmark items, 2,281 retrieval passages from pre- and post-reform Wikipedia revisions and dated templates, and old-to-new tables that a released script rebuilds from the official conversion table (not redistributed). Changed items store the pre-reform answer (except 121 address items), a $2\times2$ design uses untouched units as controls, and 400 addresses appear without diacritics. The tables agree with an independent lineage on 10,564 of 10,565 official pairs, disagreements were settled against the resolutions, and the probing-track scorer agrees with both authors' blind labels (Cohen's $κ=0.91$ and $0.89$). Data, outputs, code, and a datasheet are released. In the probing track, at least 20 of 67 models know the reform. Fourteen of them give the new province alone for at most 11% of plain questions about old addresses (old province only: 14-100%); a bare date recovers up to 100%. Post hoc (80 province-free addresses, single runs), with reasoning off, today's date in the system prompt leaves 68-100% old-province-only answers for 10 of 12 models; with reasoning, an "as of" prefix in the question leaves none for seven of eight. On the 1,262-item benchmark subset, with same-source old and new passages, small models unaware of the reform give 14-30% outdated answers and the five benchmark-track knowers (also differing in size or provider) 0-5%.

查看完整摘要
Claim verification is the task of deciding whether a source text supports a claim, contradicts it, or lacks sufficient evidence. Natural Language Inference (NLI) models can judge whether a single sentence supports or contradicts a claim, but they struggle when the evidence is spread across several sentences. Conversely, Large Language Models (LLMs) can reason across sentences, but may draw conclusions the text does not support. In this experimental study, we test whether a conservative fusion rule, labeling a claim Support or Contradict only when both models agree and defaulting to Not Enough Evidence otherwise, combines their strengths. We construct a benchmark of 2,035 claims from SciFact and CliniFact (858 Support, 152 Contradict, 1,025 Not Enough Evidence). We retrieve the top five most relevant sentences per claim using a hybrid of semantic and lexical (BM25) retrieval, which slightly outperforms either method alone. These retrieved sentences are then provided to every verification model. We then compare a DeBERTa-v3-base-mnli-fever-anli NLI baseline against Llama 3.1 8B, Qwen3 8B, and Mistral 7B. Qwen3 achieves the highest Macro-F1 (0.7937), followed by Llama 3.1 (0.7204) and Mistral (0.6388), while the NLI baseline reaches only 0.3157. Contrary to our hypothesis, fusion degrades performance, dropping Macro-F1 to 0.7392, 0.6730, and 0.5991, respectively. To understand this decline, we analyze agreements, disagreements, and common error patterns between the NLI and LLM verifiers. Our error analysis shows that NLI and LLM disagreements often result in correct LLM predictions being overturned.

We propose a simple, linguistically motivated approach to detecting AI-generated peer reviews based on the inherently human trait of subjectivity. We capture subjectivity at two levels: the semantic level of evaluative language and modality and the pragmatic level of speech acts. Using features derived from speech act annotations and model-predicted subjectivity scores, we train a lightweight classifier that achieves an AUROC of 0.9566 with only these linguistic features. Akram Elbouanani, Yousra Taleb, Gianluca Demartini, Adrian Popescu

查看完整摘要
Online political discussions increasingly involve AI-mediated participation, as users invoke LLMs to assess contested claims, arbitrate disputes, and respond to opposing viewpoints. As these systems become participants in online exchanges, they may alter which speakers and argumentative strategies receive validation within the discussion. We study this interactional dimension by reconstructing 5,376 Debate.org debates as LLM conversations and running nearly 150,000 controlled multi-turn conversations that vary user political orientation, expertise, rhetorical strategy, and defended position, including conversations with competing users. Across naturalistic and controlled debates, we find that LLMs are systematically more receptive to left-leaning than right-leaning users. The asymmetry persists when users with different political orientations defend the same position and when opposing users appear in the same conversation, indicating that it is not fully explained by stance agreement or simple accommodation to the current speaker. Repeated interaction increases receptiveness without eliminating this ordering; expertise also matters, while rhetorical differences are smaller. The pattern appears across multiple models, but is not fixed: fine-tuning on different political corpora can largely erase or reverse it. These results show that LLMs can respond systematically differently to participants in the same political debate, making such interactional asymmetries important for understanding their emerging role in online public discourse.

We introduce Omni-Life, a benchmark grounded in real-world user needs, deployment scenarios, and device-dependent observations. It combines controlled video synthesis for hazardous situations and edge cases with annotated real-world videos under a shared temporal protocol. The benchmark contains 400 videos: 290 requiring assistance, with 555 required intervention events, and 110 requiring silence throughout to test whether models can withhold unnecessary responses when no assistance is needed. We evaluate twelve configurations featuring mainstream real-time omnimodal models, jointly assessing event coverage, response content, timing, and unwanted output. Recursively self-improving (RSI) systems are increasingly viewed as a promising means of accelerating progress toward artificial general intelligence. They aim to autonomously improve their capabilities by refining their working pipelines and recursively improving the strategies that guide subsequent refinements, with gains generalizing to unseen scenarios.However, existing evaluations lack reliable metrics to distinguish capability gains from scenario fitting, sustainable and extensible protocols for unseen tasks and difficulties, and fine-grained attribution of improvement to evolution traces. As streaming generation becomes the dominant serving paradigm for both text-only and multimodal large language models, ensuring safety throughout the generation process has become a critical challenge. We introduce PGuard, a native, streaming, and plug-in guardrail that transforms safety monitoring from an external serving dependency into an integrated capability of the backbone model. PGuard directly leverages the backbone's intermediate representations for token-level safety detection, without altering its original inference pathway or generation behavior. Specifically, dynamic layer gating selectively aggregates residual-stream representations across layers to extract safety-relevant signals during both prefill and autoregressive decoding. To balance detection accuracy and computational efficiency, PGuard supports multiple representation-modeling architectures, including a lightweight stateless variant and sequence-aware variants built on multi-scale causal EMA-attention and GRU-based recurrent modules. Its design also accounts for the execution characteristics of LLM serving frameworks, such as SGLang, optimizing computation and data access to minimize additional runtime and synchronization overhead. Extensive evaluations across more than 30 benchmarks spanning text and multimodal safety, jailbreaks, cyber and CBRN risks, multilingual scenarios, and real-world traffic demonstrate state-of-the-art performance among native guardrails. On text queries with Qwen3.5-9B, PGuard achieves an average F1 score of 0.836, approaching dedicated external guardrails, while supporting deployment-time configuration of category-specific thresholds and enforcement actions.

Prompt compression is aimed at reducing input prompt lengths to enable cheaper, faster, and potentially higher-quality LLM predictions. However, existing prompt compression methods are often limited by modest compression gains, a risk of hallucination, and/or high compression latency. We propose EffComp, an Efficient prompt Compression framework for RAG-based question answering (QA). EffComp employs BERT-style document reranker and sentence selector models to allow fast extractive prompt compression at the sentence level. 2/10
MicroMouse: Masking Harmful Supervision in Long CoT via Relative Potential
Xiaowei Zhu, Hengzhu Tang, Zhenyu Zhang, Shuohuan Wang, Yubing Ren, Yanan Cao
个性化推荐理由:

该论文聚焦于长链式思维（CoT）中的有害监督屏蔽与相对势能方法，属于LLM训练安全与推理质量控制，而非RecSys/Search/Ads的核心排序或建模问题。虽然长CoT推理技术间接可用于推荐理由生成或查询理解，但论文主题偏向安全对齐与训练数据净化，与用户关注的重点方向关联很弱。

2026-11-02 08:03:52 | arXiv:2611.02710v1 |
cs.CL
查看完整摘要
Even successful long CoTs may contain harmful supervision, motivating the need for methods that can automatically identify and mask such signals during SFT. Inspired by the MicroMouse maze-solving process, we view a long CoT as a search trajectory from the input problem toward the target answer. This perspective motivates maintaining a goal-relative measure, analogous to a MicroMouse's spatial coordinates, to quantify how close the current reasoning state is to the target answer. Building on this perspective, we propose MicroMouse, an automated selective-SFT framework for masking harmful supervision in long CoTs. In MicroMouse we introduce a Relative Potential (REP) to measure how closely each reasoning state supports the gold answer. Unlike the perplexity of the gold answer alone, REP measures relative support by contrasting the gold answer with a matched foil. This contrast cancels likelihood variations shared by the pair, yielding a more stable signal of progress toward the target answer. By tracking the REP trajectory, MicroMouse identifies consecutive potential decreases as erroneous exploration and post-peak reasoning as redundant wandering, and masks these harmful supervision during SFT. Extensive experiments across multiple student model scales and reasoning benchmarks show that MicroMouse consistently outperforms Full-CoT SFT and existing selective-SFT baselines, yielding average relative gains of 5.5% in greedy accuracy and 2.6% in Pass@1 relative to Full-CoT SFT. Meanwhile, MicroMouse improves reasoning efficiency by reducing average output length by 8.8%, while identifying harmful supervision in only 1.7 seconds per long CoT on average.

cs.CLcs.AI
查看完整摘要
Large language models are increasingly consulted for religious knowledge, including the recall of specific Hadith (recorded sayings and actions of the Prophet Muhammad, peace be upon him). Unlike open-ended generation, Hadith recall is a high-stakes, verifiable task: a reference (collection, chapter, and Hadith number) has exactly one correct text, and any deviation from it constitutes a fabrication that can be mistaken for authentic scripture. This study introduces a bilingual (English/Arabic) benchmark that probes five open-weight, locally-deployable language models (Mistral-7B, Gemma3-4B, Jais-7B, ALLaM-7B, and Phi4-mini-3.8B) across all six canonical Sunni Hadith collections (Sahih al-Bukhari, Sahih Muslim, Sunan Abi Da'ud, Jami' al-Tirmidhi, Sunan an-Nasa'i, Sunan Ibn Majah), sampling 500 Hadith per collection (3,000 per model per language, 30,000 responses total). Each model is given only the bibliographic reference and asked to reproduce the exact Hadith text, with no retrieval augmentation, and is scored on lexical fidelity (BLEU) and semantic fidelity (multilingual cosine similarity), with refusals flagged separately. Across all five models and both languages, 0% of 30,000 responses achieve both high lexical and high semantic fidelity, i.e., an exact, verifiable match to the source text. Arabic responses fare uniformly worse than English. These findings suggest that current open-weight LLMs, used without retrieval grounding, are unreliable and potentially dangerous sources for exact religious-text recall, and that deployment for Islamic scholarship should mandate retrieval-augmented generation over a verified corpus rather than parametric memory. The complete code, prompts, evaluation pipeline, and scored outputs are publicly available at https://github.com/dayam8696/Hadith-Hallucination-Benchmark.

Yet major benchmarks are English-centred or translated, focus mainly on negative stereotypes, and may miss linguistic and cultural variation. We investigate gender, racial, and religious bias using 12,000 locally written Reddit posts in English, Spanish, French, and German. Only search terms are translated, preserving local context. FindLink：学习从源依据前提中评估科学联系

FindLink: Learning to Assess Scientific Connections from Source-Grounded Premises
Jingxuan Wei, Song Tan, Xiaohan Liu, Chao Sang, Changwei Wang, Linzhuang Sun, Bi...
个性化推荐理由:

该论文聚焦于从有依据的前提中评估科学联系，本质上是面向科学文献的知识/关联抽取与链接预测任务，属于纯NLP/科学知识图谱范畴，与RecSys、Search或Ads的核心问题无直接关联。虽然其中可能涉及图或链接预测技术，但缺乏明确的检索/推荐/广告应用动机，因此相关性很低。

2026-11-02 05:15:38 | arXiv:2611.02545v1 |
cs.CL
查看完整摘要
Scientific discovery from the literature requires assessing connections among findings obtained under different study conditions. Source attribution makes individual findings traceable, but does not determine what they jointly support. We introduce GI-TraceBench, a benchmark of 10,282 questions in gastrointestinal oncology that unifies provenance question answering, knowledge discovery, and hypothesis evaluation through shared Paper, Claim, and Evidence Span records. We further propose FindLink, a two-stage framework for provenance-grounded scientific reasoning. Premise Grounding constructs source-bound premises with explicit study context and support status. Structured Reasoning organizes these premises into support plans that represent both supported relations and unresolved conditions, then generates evidence-conditioned answers using deterministic compiler feedback. The compiler verifies structural and source-binding consistency, but does not assess scientific entailment. On the 1,545-question fixed-evidence test set, FindLink improves the balanced family macro score from 67.95 to 87.25 over direct supervised fine-tuning of the same backbone. A retrained variant without Structured Reasoning achieves slightly higher premise-identifier F1, but substantially lower relation and evidence-sufficiency F1. These results indicate that scientific reasoning requires not only identifying grounded premises, but also assessing what connections those premises support.

排序但未停止：从类型化决策模型的输出概率中检测提示注入劫持

2/10
Ranked but Not Stopped: Detecting Prompt-Injection Hijacks from Typed Decision Models' Output Probabilities
Junwei Yao, Jianan Liu, Yongxi Zhou, Zike Yang
个性化推荐理由:

该论文聚焦于LLM安全领域的提示注入攻击检测（安全/隐私相关话题），属于明确排除的无关主题。虽然其方法涉及决策模型输出概率分析，可能对LLM系统鲁棒性有参考价值，但与RecSys/Search/Ads的排序、推荐或搜索核心问题无直接关联，也不属于可应用于这些领域的使能技术。

2026-11-02 04:49:25 | arXiv:2611.02522v1 |
cs.CLcs.CR
查看完整摘要
Programs that use a language model as a classifier act on its most probable label. Text injected into the input can move that label to one the attacker chooses, a hijack. A monitor that does not know the attacker's label can read the model's own confidence and raise an alarm when the top probability is low. We test this monitor on AG News topic classification with five systems, three of them served through a provider's API. Pooled over five families of injected text, low confidence ranks hijacked answers above clean ones on four of the five systems (AUROC 0.86-0.90). Ranking them does not stop many of them. A threshold set so that at most 5% of clean answers to separate calibration articles alarm falls among the least confident clean answers, and every more confident hijack passes. In our main gate run on the three API-served systems, the monitor misses 51-70% of the hijacks caused by a plain injected note (per-system point estimates). When the note adds one handwritten sentence that calls the target certain and asks for full probability, the monitor misses 99-100% of the hijacks, although low confidence still ranks them at AUROC 0.70-0.79. Confidence monitors should be tested at their operating alarm rate, against attackers who claim certainty.

利用LSIRM诊断语言模型基准中的模型-项目交互

2/10
Diagnosing Model--Item Interactions in Language Model Benchmarks with LSIRM
Jinwen Luo, Han Jiang, Ziang Xiao, Susu Zhang
个性化推荐理由:

该论文将心理测量学中的潜在空间项目反应模型（LSIRM）应用于诊断语言模型基准测试，属于纯粹的LLM评估/基准分析方法。这与RecSys、Search、Ads的核心进展没有直接关联，也不提供可迁移的建模技术。除非将其用户-项目交互建模思路反向类比到推荐评估，否则与我的关注点距离较远。

2026-11-02 02:54:28 | arXiv:2611.02432v1 |
cs.CLcs.LGstat.ME
查看完整摘要
Language models with similar benchmark accuracy can succeed on different items. Overall scores do not show where their performance differs or which item properties might explain those differences. We demonstrate how the latent space item response model (LSIRM) can help evaluators investigate these patterns and their consequences for model comparison. We analyze 1,348 language models on 5,761 BBH items and compare ways of presenting and scoring answers across BBH, GPQA, MMLU-Pro, and MuSR. We examine LSIRM maps, which summarize differences in performance across models and items, alongside item properties and records of correct and incorrect responses. In BBH, nearby items on these maps often share the same correct answer, such as "Yes" or "No." Across the four benchmarks, this pattern is stronger when answers are scored using option labels, such as A, B, C, and D, than when using the text of the options. We then estimate each model's tendency to perform better or worse on items with particular correct answers and quantify how this tendency contributes to its score. This separates an estimated contribution associated with correct answers from the remaining differences in item performance. Two models' total numbers of correct answers differed by only 14 out of 5,761 items. Excluding the estimated answer-category contribution from their scores moved their ranks hundreds of places in opposite directions, while keeping all other estimates unchanged. The approach helps evaluators identify differences hidden by overall accuracy and understand how the choice of what to include in a score affects model rankings.

超越准确率：量化如何影响编码智能体

2/10
Beyond Accuracy: How Quantization Affects Coding Agents
Areej Mehboob, Muhammad Ali Shafique, Muhammad Usman Qadeer, Hamza Farooq
个性化推荐理由:

该论文研究量化对编码智能体（coding agents）的影响，属于LLM压缩/效率与智能体评估范畴，而非搜索、推荐或广告系统中的核心应用。虽然量化是Transformer效率的通用技术，但本文聚焦于代码生成智能体的准确率分析，未涉及RecSys/Search/Ads场景，也非新颖架构贡献。与我的关注点仅有非常间接的关联。

2026-11-02 01:30:27 | arXiv:2611.02368v1 |
cs.CLcs.AI
查看完整摘要
Agents are increasingly used for coding tasks, but the inference cost of large language models (LLMs) limits where they can run. Quantization reduces model size, but its effects on coding agents that execute and revise programs remain underexplored. We compare BF16 with 4-bit AWQ and GPTQ for Qwen2.5-Coder-Instruct (7B, 14B, and 32B) and Devstral-Small-2507 (24B) on OJBench coding problems across three seeds. We detect no statistically significant accuracy loss for any of the coding agents. However, final accuracy can conceal substantial behavioral changes: AWQ reduces Qwen-7B one-shot solves from 17 to 0 while increasing loop-based solves from 10 to 25. All Qwen-32B successes involve tool use, whereas Devstral rarely makes a valid executor call. Failure profiles also differ: Qwen-32B produces far more compilation errors than the smaller Qwen models, rising slightly under quantization, while Devstral's failures shift significantly from runtime errors toward wrong answers and timeouts. These findings indicate that quantization robustness in coding agents should be assessed through solution trajectories, tool use, and failure modes, in addition to final accuracy.

2026-11-02 01:00:32 | arXiv:2611.02353v1 |
cs.CLcs.HC
查看完整摘要
Generating visualisations from natural language requests (text-to-vis) enables users to explore data more efficiently. While LLMs can complete simple visualisation requests, their performance on challenging ones is not well covered by existing benchmarks. We introduce VisCodeBench, an end-to-end text-to-vis benchmark with 2,672 samples from three different domains: educational, scientific, and general audience scenarios with varying levels of complexity. We benchmark three visualisation systems---Codex, Claude Code, and LIDA---paired with a range of open and commercial LLMs across seven metrics covering execution, chart content, and visual quality. While basic execution is near-solved for strong systems, no system comes close to reproducing the reference data, with the best displayed data similarity reaching only $0.81$ and falling to $0.69$ on our hardest subset for this metric. We further find that frontier models separate on chart correctness and efficiency, with ClaudeCode-Opus-4.7 improving chart correctness by around 5 points over Codex-GPT-5 while running roughly 20 seconds faster. Separately, we find that system design matters as much as the underlying model: despite both running on GPT-5, Codex clearly outperforms LIDA, showing that the surrounding prompting, execution, and refinement pipeline can shape outcomes as much as the model itself. Finally, latency remains a practical obstacle, as even the strongest systems take 39-78 seconds to produce results. VisCodeBench is available at \href{https://github.com/SAIPLL/VisCodeBench}{https://github.com/SAIPLL/VisCodeBench} and accessed via a leaderboard; the full dataset is kept private to prevent LLM training use.

一种投降方向随规模出现，并在验证强的地方具有因果承载作用：八个模型、两个家族与五项测量风险

A Capitulation Direction Emerges With Scale and Is Causally Load-Bearing Where Validation Is Strong: Eight Models, Two Families, and Five Measurement Hazards
Saad Aamir, Muhammad Awais Bin Adil
个性化推荐理由:

该标题聚焦于模型规模扩展下的内部表征现象（"投降方向"）及其因果作用，属于LLM可解释性与基础行为分析，未涉及RecSys、搜索或广告的任何信号。虽然"随规模出现"的趋势对理解LLM能力演化有间接参考价值，但缺乏对推荐/搜索/广告系统的直接应用或可迁移的技术贡献，且测量风险讨论偏向纯NLP评估议题。

2026-11-02 00:09:34 | arXiv:2611.02318v1 |
cs.CLcs.LG
查看完整摘要
Sycophancy in language models is well documented as a behaviour but poorly localised as a mechanism. We ask whether capitulation under user pushback, the abandonment of a correct answer when a user objects, corresponds to a linear direction in the residual stream, and whether such a direction emerges with scale. Across eight instruction-tuned models spanning 0.5B to 14B parameters in two families, under a pre-registered protocol with leave-one-question-out cross-validation, within-question permutation nulls, and a positive control, we find no usable direction below 7B and a direction that clears our validation gate at 7B, 8B and 14B. Ablating that direction in Qwen2.5-7B reduces the rate at which the model reverses a correct answer by 4.8 percentage points, roughly a quarter in relative terms, while the identical intervention on a gate-failing direction and on a marginally passing one produces no effect. Probe quality, not statistical significance, predicts causal relevance. Behaviourally, pushback destroys three to five times more correct answers than it repairs at 1B and reaches parity by 7B, falsifying our own pre-registered prediction, and a sign-reversing crossover between pushback types survives an order of magnitude of scale in Qwen and holds at 8B in Llama, with marginalisation over five paraphrases per type. We additionally quantify five measurement hazards, including a numerical-precision effect that changes roughly one episode label in ten.

GRIPS: 面向SQL生成的预SQL接地信息的实证研究

GRIPS: An Empirical Study of Pre-SQL Grounding Information for SQL Generation
Ekata Mitra, Yufei Tao, Kamalamalini Nagasundaram, Ameeta Agrawal
个性化推荐理由:

该论文研究的是Text-to-SQL生成中的grounding信息，属于自然语言到结构化查询的转换，与推荐系统、搜索或广告的排序/召回核心问题关联较弱。虽然SQL生成可类比于结构化搜索查询理解，但论文聚焦于数据库查询生成而非用户意图检索或排序建模，与我的当前关注方向仅有非常间接的联系。

2026-11-02 00:03:50 | arXiv:2611.02314v1 |
cs.CL
查看完整摘要
Text-to-SQL systems answer natural language questions over structured data, but can fail before SQL generation by linking question mentions to the wrong table, column, value, or relation. Even valid SQL can then return an incorrect answer. This raises a central question: What pre-SQL information helps a SQL generator, and when does additional information cease to help? We investigate this question on TempTabQA-C and BIRD. Among the audited SQL Static failures, grounding errors form the largest category on both benchmarks. We introduce GRIPS (Grounding Representation and Input Processing for SQL generation), a three-stage method that derives structured pre-SQL information to make these decisions explicit before query generation. Across GPT-5-nano and Llama-70B, GRIPS-Adaptive improves over SQL Static in 18 of 20 settings, with an average accuracy gain of 11.8 percentage points, while reducing model-call token usage by 47.8%. Our controlled analysis shows which grounding information can improve SQL generation, whereas adding more pre-SQL information does not consistently help. By separating question-input grounding from SQL generation, GRIPS makes text-to-SQL more accurate, efficient, and easier to diagnose.

Concise-9B：消除推理模型思维中的冗余

2/10
Concise-9B: Removing the Slack from a Reasoning Model's Thinking
Alexander Boesgaard Lorup
个性化推荐理由:

该论文聚焦于提升推理模型（reasoning model）的思维效率，去除冗余推理步骤，属于纯LLM推理效率优化，未涉及推荐、搜索或广告场景。虽然更高效的推理模型可能间接降低RecSys/Ads中LLM部署成本，但论文本身与我的核心关注点（如Transformer效率改进、LLM在RecSys/搜索/广告中的直接应用、VLM类比等）关联很弱。

2026-11-01 23:02:43 | arXiv:2611.02278v1 |
cs.LGcs.CL
查看完整摘要
Reasoning models answer better when they think longer, but a thinking trace keeps going well after its answer has formed. We measure this slack in Qwen3.5-9B and remove most of it with concise self-training: two rounds of on-policy rejection-sampling fine-tuning that keep the shortest correct samples, plus a small share of answer-synthesis targets, giving Concise-9B. On 8,983 held-out problems (competition and hard mathematics, SuperGPQA, a five-area confirmation set and MMLU-Pro), Concise-9B generates 0.66x the tokens of Qwen3.5-9B at a 32K thinking budget, with accuracy equivalent within +/-1 point (-0.20 [-0.77, +0.36] points). Forcing each trace on the hard and confirmation sets to answer every 2,048 thinking tokens, we find that 54-64% of Qwen3.5-9B's thinking tokens come after the point from which every forced answer equals the final one (41-48% for Concise-9B). Stopping there would keep every answer at 0.35-0.44x the base model's tokens, but finding that point takes hindsight: stopping when three consecutive forced answers agree costs the base model 2.4-9.2 points. Concise self-training removes slack without that cost: 86% [83, 90] of the thinking tokens it saves come after the answer has formed. It has a limit: with traces continued to 80K tokens, Concise-9B solves 25 fewer of 1,276 hard problems (-25 [-49, -1]), mostly in competition mathematics. The measurement also yields a cheap commit signal for adaptive sampling that matched two-sample escalation within +/-1 point at 0.67x its tokens in a pre-registered test. Results on Qwen3.5-4B, on which the recipe was developed, and a replication on Olmo-3-7B-Think suggest that the saving depends on how verbose the base model is.

EgoActionBench：面向第一人称人类视频的密集动作描述基准测试

EgoActionBench: Benchmarking Dense Action Captioning for Egocentric Human Videos
Yujiao Shen, Jiahe Song, Haitian Li, Jingkang Yang, Ziwei Liu

该论文聚焦于第一人称视频的密集动作描述基准构建，属于以评估基准为核心的纯视觉/多模态理解任务，与推荐系统、搜索或广告的核心排序建模无直接关联。虽然视频理解技术理论上可迁移到用户行为序列建模，但本文作为基准测试工作，并未提出可复用的建模方法或架构创新，应用潜力有限。

2026-11-02 18:59:29 | arXiv:2611.03646v1 |
cs.CV
查看完整摘要
Learning from human data offers a scalable path toward embodied intelligence, yet it requires models to accurately understand fine-grained human actions from egocentric observations. However, existing benchmarks remain limited in action atomicity, temporal precision, and hand-action association. We introduce EgoActionBench, a benchmark that represents continuous human manipulation as temporally localized atomic actions associated with the left hand, right hand, or both hands. EgoActionBench contains 1,407 episodes with 41,132 human-refined action annotations. To evaluate such dense predictions, we propose an evaluation protocol that retrieves temporally eligible candidates, selects a prediction by content agreement, and scores semantic fidelity and temporal overlap alongside action precision and recall, jointly capturing what happens, when it happens, and which hand performs it. We benchmark 20 baseline models and systems on EgoActionBench and further adapt two backbones, Qwen3-VL-4B and LLaVA-OneVision-2-8B, through supervised fine-tuning followed by reinforcement learning on automatically annotated training episodes. EgoAction improves EgoActionScore by 15.9 and 23.2 points on the two backbones, and both adapted models outperform all open-source baselines. Our experiments reveal substantial limitations of current models in action atomicity, temporal precision, and hand-action association, highlighting the gap between general video understanding and the action understanding required for embodied intelligence.

Reviso：在视频字幕生成中并非所有视觉证据都应获得同等关注

2/10
Reviso: Not All Visual Evidence Deserves Equal Attention in Video Captioning
Tengfei Liu, Yang Shi, Yuran Wang, Yuqi Tang, Yizhuo Jia, Zhuoran Zhang, Qixun W...
个性化推荐理由:

该论文聚焦视频字幕生成中的视觉注意力分配，属于多模态内容生成（VLM/字幕）方向，与推荐、搜索、广告的核心排序或检索问题无直接关联。其注意力重加权思想理论上可类比到多模态融合，但缺乏对RecSys/Search/Ads场景的明确迁移或实验验证，故相关度低。

2026-11-02 18:41:14 | arXiv:2611.03602v1 |
cs.CV
查看完整摘要
Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) can generate detailed video captions, yet they may overemphasize minor details while overlooking essential context and key events. We argue that not all visual evidence deserves equal attention, as the importance of visual information depends on the content of each video. Motivated by this observation, we propose Reviso, an agentic video captioning framework that learns what to observe and how to gather relevant visual evidence. From a low-frame-rate global preview, Reviso constructs a hierarchical Context--Focus--Points (CFP) observation plan that identifies the video context, prioritizes key aspects, and defines concrete observation targets and corresponding tools. Guided by this plan, Reviso performs goal-directed active perception by selectively revisiting temporal segments and combining tools for fine-grained inspection. It interprets the acquired evidence, updates its plan as needed, and integrates global and local information to generate the final caption. To learn this observation strategy, we develop a verification-guided pipeline that constructs high-quality training trajectories through factual verification and teacher-guided refinement. We further train Reviso using Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO), incorporating a key-fact reward for caption quality, a Plan Alignment Reward (PAR) for identifying important observation targets, and an Unproductive Tool-Call Penalty (UTCP) that adjusts observation-cost penalties based on caption quality. Experiments across four video captioning benchmarks show that Reviso outperforms the evaluated open-source baselines, while its generated captions better support downstream video question answering. These findings highlight the effectiveness and potential of priority-guided active observation for video captioning.

结构化视觉景观：在生物与人工神经网络中生成偏好表征

2026-11-02 18:39:55 | arXiv:2611.03597v1 |
cs.CVcs.LGcs.NE
查看完整摘要
Understanding how neurons respond to visual stimulus is an important question in both deep learning and neuroscience. It has significant implications for enhancing the interpretability of black-box artificial neural networks and for understanding the visual representation in the human brain. We propose a structured visual representations landscape and design an activation-based prior that allows us to effectively regularize the landscape with activations from brain regions or neural network units. Our model Vis-Lens integrates a variational autoencoder and a diffusion model into an image generation framework. It enables the generation of natural, realistic preferred images by directly modifying the activation-regularized latent codes without per-image iterative optimization. We demonstrate the effectiveness of our framework on both artificial neural networks and human visual cortex using functional magnetic resonance imaging (fMRI) data. Our framework improves activation modulation and image realism compared with a recent state-of-the-art method for generating preferred visual representations of these neural targets.

cs.CV
查看完整摘要
Foundational vision models have become the de facto standard for many vision tasks due to their strong performance. However, they are notoriously opaque and remain hard to interpret. We present ALOE (ALign Once to Explain), a one-time, label-free feature alignment approach that efficiently converts foundational vision models into inherently interpretable B-cos variants. Once aligned, the B-cos backbone is used as a drop-in replacement across several downstream tasks-amortizing the cost of interpretability. ALOE is robust across pre-training paradigms (supervised, self-supervised, vision-language) and can preserve teacher-level generalization with orders of magnitude less data than training from scratch. On classification, it strongly outperforms vanilla B-cosification (e.g., +10.5 p.p. top-1 on ImageNet for DINOv3 ViT-B/16), retains strong linear probing, k-NN, and zero-shot transfer competitive with foundational backbones (DINOv3, SigLIP2) across diverse downstream datasets. It also preserves spatially structured features useful for dense prediction, while yielding well-localized and highly human-interpretable explanations by design.

ArabGeoText: Tool-Augmented Vision-Language Models for Arabic Geospatial Text Spotting

cs.CV
查看完整摘要
Geospatial text spotting involves locating and transcribing geographic labels, such as road names, district names, and landmarks, in raster maps. These labels are often small, densely arranged, and arbitrarily oriented. Although Arabic text recognition has been studied extensively in documents and natural scenes, Arabic geospatial text spotting remains underexplored. We introduce ARABGEOTEXT, an Arabic-centric benchmark covering 970 geographic locations in Riyadh and Jeddah, with 17,875 manually annotated text instances per view type. For each location, we pair an OpenStreetMap (OSM) rendering with a composite created by overlaying that rendering on the corresponding remote sensing (RS) imagery. The paired views preserve label content and pixel geometry, allowing evaluation across different backgrounds. Geographic partitions support in-city and city-disjoint evaluation of detection, recognition, and end-to-end spotting. Experiments with direct Qwen3.5-4B spotting show difficulties in recovering all labels within a tile. We therefore propose a tool-augmented vision-language framework that combines YOLO26 localization and crop rectification with Qwen3.5-4B recognition adapted through low-rank adaptation (LoRA). The framework outperforms direct LoRA-adapted Qwen3.5-4B spotting across both image views and test cities. With localization held fixed, reader adaptation further improves recognition over the pretrained zero-shot reader. The dataset and code will be made publicly available at https://github.com/yakoubbazi/ArabGeoText.

R²-Omni：减少全模态大语言模型长文本生成中的结构与精度冗余

2/10
R$^2$-Omni: Reducing Structural and Precision Redundancy in Omni-LLMs for Long-Form Generation
Bolong Feng, Pengfei Zhou, Zhenglin Wan, Wei Wang, Wangbo Zhao
个性化推荐理由:

该论文聚焦于全模态LLM长文本生成中的结构与精度冗余削减，属于LLM效率优化的通用技术，虽可能对推荐/搜索/广告场景中的长序列生成任务有间接效率提升潜力，但缺乏对RecSys/Search/Ads领域的直接针对性。总体与当前关注焦点的直接关联度较低。

2026-11-02 14:57:48 | arXiv:2611.03247v1 |
cs.CV
查看完整摘要
Omni-modal large language models (Omni-LLMs) typically process large numbers of multimodal tokens from audio-visual inputs, incurring substantial computational costs and a large memory footprint for the key-value (KV) cache. Aggressive token pruning can alleviate this problem while maintaining competitive performance on multiple-choice tasks, which require only a single option as output. However, we observe substantial quality degradation when generating long-form, open-ended responses. We attribute this gap to query-guided selection, which can retain sufficient evidence for answer selection while discarding details needed for comprehensive generation. This motivates reducing overlapping information across tokens, termed structural redundancy, while preserving diverse content. However, the retained representations may still contain precision redundancy due to unnecessarily high numerical precision in their cached values. To address these two levels of redundancy in token representations, we introduce a training-free framework named R$^2$-Omni. First, Structure-Aware Token Consolidation (STC) consolidates redundant representations using spatiotemporal similarity in video and local continuity in audio. Then, Semantic-Sensitivity Guided Precision Allocation (SSPA) allocates value precision to the retained representations according to the estimated reduction in attention-output error under quantized keys, with query attention and audio-visual alignment providing a semantic prior. Extensive experiments on long-form generation benchmarks demonstrate an average 5.0$\times$ reduction in the memory footprint while preserving 97.98% of baseline generation quality and achieving a 1.48$\times$ end-to-end speedup, highlighting the benefits of jointly reducing structural and precision redundancy.

2/10
Calibrating Multimodal Test-Time Adaptation under Reliability and Availability Modality Shifts
Jiangmeng Li, Jinglin Xu, Yi Li, Yumeng Liu, Jiahuan Zhou, Fanjiang Xu, Hui Xion...
个性化推荐理由:

该论文研究多模态模型在测试时遇到模态可靠性/可用性偏移时的自适应校准问题，属于多模态鲁棒性/测试时自适应方向，虽与VLM类比异构数据建模有微弱概念联系，但未涉及推荐、搜索或广告场景，也无明确的应用潜力说明。

2026-11-02 14:48:39 | arXiv:2611.03232v1 |
cs.CV
查看完整摘要
Multimodal test-time adaptation (TTA) enables pretrained multimodal models to accommodate distribution shifts during inference using only unlabeled test data, without recourse to the original training data. Compared with unimodal adaptation, multimodal adaptation is further complicated by the coexistence and interaction of multiple representation perspectives. This complexity is particularly pronounced under modality reliability shifts, which preserve modality availability but degrade information quality, and modality availability shifts, which render the corresponding modality entirely inaccessible. Category-conditional Gaussian modeling offers a natural starting point for handling such distributional variations, having proven effective in unimodal test-time adaptation for characterizing representation distributions, yet its extension to multimodal settings requires a more elaborate formulation. To this end, we propose Adaptive Probabilistic Gaussian Calibration (AdaPGC), which extends category-conditional Gaussian modeling across modality-specific and multimodal fusion representations for probabilistic calibration under multimodal distribution shifts. AdaPGC continuously updates category-conditional Gaussian models over modality-specific and fusion representations, using the resulting probabilistic predictions to calibrate source model predictions and guide reliability based rectification, while exploiting their joint distributions to recover unavailable fusion representations. Extensive experiments under diverse modality corruption and missingness conditions demonstrate that AdaPGC effectively improves robustness under both modality reliability and availability shifts. Our source code will be released at https://github.com/XuJinglinn/AdaPGC-plus.

Diffusion models are evolving from general tools into personalized systems that adapt to user needs through fine-tuning and guidance. However, diffusion optimization mainly targets measurable objectives such as aesthetics, rarely examining whether they align with human intent. Human needs are multidimensional, contextual, and dynamic, whereas existing methods approximate them with fixed metrics. Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) have shown strong potential in robotic surgery for question answering, scene understanding, and grounded assistance, but their high computational cost limits real clinical deployment. Knowledge distillation provides a practical way to build compact surgical MLLMs, yet direct distillation is not well suited to surgical data because surgical samples vary greatly in difficulty, visual ambiguity, and required clinical context, making uniform teacher-student transfer unstable. In addition, the role of supervision changes during training: early stages require stronger teacher guidance for multimodal alignment, while later stages rely more on task supervision for effective optimization. To address these challenges, we propose SurgPace, a complexity-aware continuous curriculum framework for progressive distillation of surgical vision-language models. This design separates sample-level curriculum from objective-level scheduling, leading to more stable and effective distillation. We evaluate SurgPace on Surg-396K, a benchmark built from four public surgical datasets and covering five dialogue paradigms. SurgPace consistently outperforms strong MLLM distillation baselines, demonstrating its effectiveness for building compact, memory-efficient surgical MLLMs with faster decoding under controlled benchmarks. Code is available at https://github.com/hezhicheng2002/SurgPace.

Balancing Representations by Identifying and Generating Under-represented Data
Filipe Laitenberger, Hirokatsu Kataoka, Yuki Asano, Cees Snoek, Mohammadreza Sal...

2026-11-02 11:17:31 | arXiv:2611.02952v1 |
cs.CVcs.AI
查看完整摘要
Self-supervised learning inherits the coverage of its source data. When an unlabeled corpus is long-tailed, the representation becomes dense around frequent modes and thin where coverage is weak. Objectives for long-tailed SSL reweight the images already in the corpus and add no new ones. We introduce BRIDGE (Balancing Representations by Identifying and Generating Under-represented Data), a label-free feedback loop around the source set. The current encoder scores every source image by its mean kNN distance, and a budgeted acquisition rule selects images from a band of that score distribution where support is thin and the generator still produces faithful variants. A frozen image-to-image diffusion model then adds local variants of the selected anchors, and pretraining continues on the enlarged source set. We formulate acquisition as recoverability-weighted allocation of local support and derive when a finite budget should favor a sparse band that excludes the extreme tail and when reallocation after each repair is optimal under a fixed objective. Across five long-tailed, web-scraped and synthetic source regimes, BRIDGE has the highest seven-task linear-probe and kNN average of the compared methods, and paired experiments extend the gain to ViT-S under contrastive and self-distillation objectives, to low-label fine-tuning and to semantic segmentation. The linear-probe gain is smaller under masked reconstruction. Generated repair matches an oracle that restores the withheld real images, and under a frozen encoder the inserted images shrink the neighborhoods of the selected anchors about twice as much as those of matched unselected images. BRIDGE needs no labels and leaves the pretraining objective unchanged, so it can be added to pretraining on uncurated web data, where the encoder decides which images to generate.

2026-11-02 10:51:26 | arXiv:2611.02921v1 |
cs.CVcs.LG
查看完整摘要
Representation regularization has emerged as an effective way to improve diffusion transformer training. Yet one of the most canonical regularizers--InfoNCE-style contrastive learning--remains largely underexplored in this setting. We naturally ask a question: can standard contrastive learning serve as an effective regularizer for diffusion transformers? Surprisingly, we find that naive InfoNCE regularization brings only marginal gains. Our analysis reveals that the objective is easily dominated by a shortcut: near-orthogonal noise components in diffusion inputs provide a bypass for enlarging negative-pair distances while weakening positive-pair alignment, especially at high noise levels. To unlock the benefit of contrastive regularization, we propose Clean Representation-ANchored Contrastive (CRAN) regularization. Specifically, CRAN anchors contrastive dispersion with clean-sample representation distances, transferring clean relational semantic structure to noisy diffusion representations to suppress the noise shortcut. Extensive experiments on ImageNet show that CRAN consistently improves diffusion transformer training across model scales and training budgets, outperforming internal feature-alignment and dispersive regularization baselines while matching external DINO-guided REPA. These results re-establish InfoNCE-based contrastive learning as an effective regularizer for diffusion training, once its noise-driven over-dispersion is properly constrained.

Infrared and visible image fusion integrates complementary information from both modalities to provide a more comprehensive representation of the scene. We propose ProtoFusion, a protocol-guided generative framework for infrared and visible image fusion. Instead of relying on fixed fusion operators, hand-crafted objectives, or manually specified one-shot prompts, ProtoFusion formulates fusion as protocol-constrained generation. It represents fusion intent with a structured protocol, consisting of environment state cards, grounding principles, and a style profile, which explicitly specifies which evidence should be preserved or suppressed and which properties the fused result should satisfy. To optimize this protocol, we introduce protocol-guided prompt refinement, a closed-loop learning framework that iteratively performs generation, evaluation, and revision under multimodal evidence. Extensive experiments on standard benchmarks show that ProtoFusion improves quantitative performance, achieves favorable results in aesthetic evaluation, and produces visually coherent fusion outputs. Adapting features before upsampling can improve dense prediction, but the gains may arise from changes in feature values, spatial aggregation, or both. We investigate this ambiguity by comparing matched residual adapters before and after a frozen JAFAR upsampler under a shared parent. We construct a readout-null intervention that preserves a frozen linear classifier's coarse logits while allowing spatial attention weights to adapt. Guidance/value exchanges on trained models and retrained fixed-guidance controls test the utility of this adaptation route. In three-seed experiments on PASCAL Visual Object Classes (VOC) and Cityscapes, adaptation before upsampling improves mean intersection over union (mIoU) by 0.807 and 1.413 percentage points, respectively, over adaptation after upsampling. 查看完整摘要
Few-step autoregressive video diffusion enables efficient generation, yet maintaining consistency over long rollouts remains challenging, especially when subjects or scenes reappear after going out of sight. Despite progress in fidelity and rollout robustness, existing distillation methods do not fully exploit richer historical information to constrain the student's current prediction. To address this gap, we introduce \textbf{History Forcing}, a unified on-policy distillation framework that strengthens historical supervision for few-step autoregressive video generators. The framework uses the residual between teacher predictions with and without informative historical context to decouple this supervision. Such a historical context can be obtained using earlier generated chunks with less accumulated drift or a fixed-view teacher rollout that keeps the scene visible while the student looks away. The residual is then weighted and added to the score or velocity prediction used for distillation, forming a corrected target. By aligning the student with this corrected target through distillation, History Forcing incorporates long-range historical constraints into autoregressive generation. Our method achieves state-of-the-art performance among 1.3B models, with a VBench quality score of 85.07 for 15s video generation and a VBench-Long aggregate score of 79.82 for 60s generation. For out-of-sight world modeling, evaluations further demonstrate more plausible scene evolution and more consistent states when the camera returns, with Evolution Quality improving from 2.35 to 2.70 on PlayWorld and Object Consistency from 58.43 to 62.18 on MemoBench.

2/10
Lookahead Prediction-Guided Adaptive Visual Refocusing for Interleaved Multimodal Reasoning
Mengzhao Wang, Yanli Ji, Peng Ye
个性化推荐理由:

该论文聚焦于交错多模态推理中的视觉重聚焦机制，属于纯多模态推理方法研究，未涉及推荐、搜索或广告场景。虽然其'前瞻预测引导的注意力重聚焦'思想在理论上可类比于序列推荐中的动态兴趣聚焦，但论文本身缺乏与RecSys/Search/Ads的明确关联或可迁移的Transformer效率/架构创新。因此相关性较低。

2026-11-02 08:58:28 | arXiv:2611.02780v1 |
cs.CV
查看完整摘要
Interleaved multimodal chain-of-thought reasoning improves the visual reasoning capabilities of multimodal large language models by dynamically incorporating visual evidence during reasoning. However, existing methods primarily select evidence from historical context. When the reasoning focus shifts, history-guided evidence may remain focused on prior regions and fail to cover the visual information required by the next step. We refer to this lag between visual evidence and subsequent reasoning demands as Temporal Evidence Myopia. To address this issue, we propose Lookahead Prediction-guided Adaptive Visual Refocusing (LPAVR), a training-free test-time framework. LPAVR transforms Multi-Token Prediction (MTP) from a decoding acceleration mechanism into a lookahead signal for anticipating upcoming reasoning intent. During MTP decoding, the draft head generates multiple future tokens, while the target model verifies them in parallel and retains the longest prefix consistent with its decoding trajectory. This target-verified prefix contains near-future semantics, which LPAVR uses to construct lookahead-guided visual evidence and assess whether history-guided evidence covers the indicated regions. If the coverage is sufficient, the history-guided evidence is retained; otherwise, LPAVR switches to the lookahead-guided evidence. This better aligns the visual context with upcoming reasoning and improves consistency between draft predictions and target-model decoding. Experiments on multiple multimodal reasoning benchmarks show that LPAVR improves both reasoning accuracy and the acceptance rates of MTP speculative tokens, jointly enhancing visual reasoning and speculative decoding efficiency.

Lip reading plays a crucial role in applications such as speech recognition, assistive technologies for the hearing-impaired, and biometric authentication. However, performance often degrades when the speaker varies and when certain speech sounds are visually similar. To address these challenges, we propose LipLingo, a robust lip-reading system based on the LipNet architecture, enhanced with a standardized preprocessing pipeline for consistent mouth-region representation, a rotational validation strategy for improved generalization to unseen speakers, and a flexible similarity evaluation for more reliable sentence-level assessment. These results provide insights into strategies that enhance lip-reading robustness and highlight directions for future work toward real-world deployment.

Understanding how objects and their spatial relationships evolve in 3D space over time remains challenging for vision-language models (VLMs), particularly under changing viewpoints. Recent approaches incorporate geometric information into reasoning, but explicit supervision for temporally ordered 4D state evolution and its use as intermediate evidence remains limited. We introduce \textbf{Trace4D}, which trains VLMs to predict 4D state sequences and reason over their inter-timestep changes alongside temporal visual observations. Its 4D-grounded chain-of-thought (CoT) explicitly represents 4D states, analyzes temporal visual context, and connects both as evidence for the answer. We construct Trace4D-Train from in-the-wild videos with 600K 4D perception examples and approximately 70K grounded QA examples, and train with joint supervised fine-tuning followed by geometry-aware GRPO. Experiments on Trace4D-Bench and VLM4D show improved QA accuracy over the base VLMs and benefits from reasoning over 4D state changes and temporal visual observations.

Long-form multi-shot video editing requires an intended edit to remain coherent across abrupt changes in scene, viewpoint, and subject appearance, while processing substantially longer visual context than conventional single-shot editors. We introduce MoviEdit, an end-to-end framework for scalable long-form multi-shot video editing that jointly addresses the challenges of data, modeling, and evaluation. To provide cross-shot supervision data at scale, we develop automatic data-generation pipelines spanning synchronized multi-view footage, real-world multi-shot videos, and generated multi-shot content, yielding 87K paired editing videos without manual annotation. 面向人类的世界模型

Human-Oriented World Model

2026-11-02 07:40:04 | arXiv:2611.02690v1 |
cs.AIcs.CVcs.LG
查看完整摘要
Supporting a person over repeated interactions requires interpreting their current state in context and anticipating its subsequent evolution. We formulate the \emph{Human-Oriented World Model} (HOWM) as a conceptual framework centered on task-relevant human states and their evolution. HOWM connects current observations and individual history with environmental and social context to organize continuing state estimation, personalized dynamics prediction, and action support. Dynamic expectations and new observations jointly inform the current estimate, which in turn anchors personalized forecasts based on shared knowledge and individual experience. An external policy relates forecasts under alternative conditions to the person's goals, preferences, and constraints when considering support. Actual responses and outcomes provide evidence for revising the state estimate and personal dynamic patterns, thereby informing subsequent estimation, prediction, and support. We illustrate the framework through hypothetical training and learning scenarios. Our research agenda asks whether accumulated interaction experience improves subsequent estimation and prediction, and whether using the model helps the person pursue their goals over time.

cs.CLcs.AIcs.CYcs.DLcs.IR
查看完整摘要
PlagiaCorpus is a bilingual Arabic-English dataset for benchmarking plagiarism and text-reuse detection, including reuse produced by large language models (LLMs) and reuse across languages. It contains 198,643 text records: a source corpus of 34,885 passages drawn from English and Arabic Wikipedia and two openly licensed book collections, and 163,758 queries (147,381 for training and 16,377 for testing). The queries cover five reuse classes of increasing difficulty, namely verbatim copying, character-level obfuscation, same-language paraphrasing by two different LLMs, and English--Arabic translation with structural paraphrasing, together with a Safe class of non-reused passages. Every positive query is linked to its source passage, and the train/test split is grouped by source passage so that no passage contributes queries to both splits. The release was curated through language identification, removal of LLM generation artefacts, and exact and cross-lingual de-duplication. Technical validation reports per-class length statistics, automatic checks of the train/test split, and inter-annotator agreement on a manually rated sample. The dataset is distributed in JSON and Parquet formats under a CC BY-SA 4.0 license.

ufakzeka-kulak is an open Turkish speech-to-text model of 31.6M parameters, a FastConformer encoder with a token-and-duration transducer (TDT) and a CTC head, trained from scratch and released under Apache-2.0. It writes cased, punctuated Turkish with numbers as words. Its 4,977 training hours come from sources whose licences allow commercial use; 281 hours are human-transcribed, and for the other 4,696 the training text is an uploader subtitle or a machine transcript, kept only where a second, independent reading of the audio agrees with it. The recipe, checkpoint, labelling and export choices were made on development data, and the test sets were scored once, in a release gate fixed before training. With greedy TDT decoding the normalised word error rate (WER) is 0.0921 (95 percent interval 0.0792 to 0.1055) on FLEURS Turkish test and 0.0916 (0.0873 to 0.0958) on Common Voice 27.0 Turkish test, lower than Whisper base and Whisper small on both sets with paired intervals wholly below zero; Whisper large-v3 (greedy) scores 0.0604 and 0.0767. On conversational speech the WER is 0.3807. We also report punctuation and casing, degraded audio, and how closely each shipped export reproduces the trained model.

Law changes over time. A court decides a case under the law in force on that day, and legal QA benchmarks grade answers against today's law. We checked 156,650 decisions of four Thai courts. Up to 67% of them cite law that has since been amended or repealed. We introduce NitiBench+, the sequel of our NitiBench, a Thai legal QA benchmark with 1,670 items across 17 domains, built from decisions of three courts. For each cited section it holds both the text in force on the decision date and the current text, and its retrieval corpus holds every dated version of every Thai statute section. Answers are graded on the Thai Bar Council rubric, the Bar-standard score, and on the overlap between the cited sections and the court's. The report has two parts. Part I measures the cost of the wrong text on 13 open-weight models. Reading the current text of amended law drops the Bar-standard score by 0.25 and missing repealed law drops it by 0.79. The penalty comes from rule changes, 0.45 on the 428 rule-changed items and none on the 331 wording-only items, and Citation F1 does not see it. The text in force is the fair setting. Part II benchmarks today's models on that setting. A public leaderboard ranks 26 models on a held-out test split of 500 items stratified by domain, with a validation split of the same shape released for public use, from memory alone, with the court's sections provided, and as a retrieval agent over the law in force, with the cost per item and the hallucinated citations. The best model scores 7.78 out of 10 and the best open-weight model 6.39. Without a search tool only five open-weight models recall any Thai law, and with it twenty of the 26 models gain and six lose. Leaderboard will take new models as they are released. Legal benchmarks must track dates, and on the right text today's models still leave a wide gap on Thai law.

cs.CLcs.SE
查看完整摘要
Large language model (LLM) coding agents increasingly operate with shell access, allowing them to write scripts, move files, alter permissions, and modify system configurations. However, an agent may achieve a user's goal while causing unintended harm, such as deleting unrelated files, weakening access controls, or corrupting data, in response to benign inputs without explicit jailbreaking. This failure mode is particularly important because apparent task success may reassure users that an agent behaved correctly, causing harmful side effects to go unnoticed. We call this harmful success: the agent successfully completes the intended benign task while simultaneously causing unintended harm. Despite its importance, harmful success is difficult to measure because conventional task-success metrics overlook harmful behaviors, while existing safety evaluations are often designed solely to assess harmful outcomes. We introduce VeriHarm, an automatic framework for constructing programmatically verifiable benchmarks that directly measure harmful success. VeriHarm minimally perturbs coding tasks to elicit unintended harmful behavior while maintaining the benignity of the task and preserving the original task objective. It then synthesizes executable harm tests that, together with the original task-correctness tests, evaluate both task accuracy and harmful outcomes. Frontier agents, including GPT-5.6-Sol and Claude-Opus-5, achieve an average task accuracy of 95% on our curated VeriHarm-Bench, yet still struggle to complete tasks safely: their safe success rate, the fraction of tasks completed successfully without triggering the designated harm, averages only 37%. These results suggest that successful task completion can frequently coexist with unintended harm, and that task-success metrics alone may substantially understate the potential safety risks of coding agents.

2026-11-02 17:53:26 | arXiv:2611.03518v1 |
cs.CLcs.AIcs.LGcs.MA
查看完整摘要
Organisations increasingly deploy large language model agents that must follow organisational values. We check whether activation steering, which adds vectors to a model's internal activations during generation without changing its weights, can improve compliance beyond prompting alone. Since most scenarios involve several values at once, we focus on multi-value steering. A simple way to build these vectors is to contrast responses that follow all values relevant to a scenario with responses that violate them. However, this gives nearly identical vectors for different values: they capture a general compliance signal rather than individual values, which limits their use when combining several values. We propose Value-Isolating Activation Steering (VIAS), which contrasts responses to the same scenario under two prompts, one listing all relevant values and one omitting a single target value. We keep a pair only if the target value changes from compliant to non-compliant and both responses still meet a minimum compliance level on the other values. We evaluate VIAS on PROTECT, a workplace compliance dataset where each scenario is tagged with relevant values from a 21-value taxonomy. VIAS reduces overlap between value vectors, making them better suited for multi-value steering, although this alone does not lead to independent control over each value. Combined with prompting, VIAS complies with every tagged value on 69.6% of scenarios for Qwen2.5-7B, 75.2% for Mistral-7B, and 72.6% for Phi-3.5-mini, outperforming prompting alone for all three models.

2026-11-02 16:20:07 | arXiv:2611.03377v1 |
cs.AIcs.CLcs.CV
查看完整摘要
Every multimodal-LLM multiple-choice benchmark ends with a scoring rule that converts a model's response, often free-form text rather than a clean option letter, into a right/wrong verdict, buried in its code. Unlike the dataset, this rule is chosen without justification and left unexamined, yet it sets the published number. We reproduce the official scorers of eight peer-reviewed benchmarks on five open vision-language models (VLMs) that fit a single 16 GB GPU, and introduce a blinded human-plus-LLM-judge adjudication (Cohen's kappa=0.956) that recovers what the model actually answered. The scoring rule alone moves accuracy by up to 22 points on identical outputs, yet exactly 0 wherever a model emits clean option letters, so it hides on strong-model leaderboards. It is the fallback and not the rambling that does the damage: the benchmark with the highest non-compliance we measure has an artifact of exactly zero, because its scorer abstains instead of guessing; a reasoning model with non-compliance 0.000 still swings 9.5 points with which regex match of its trace the parser keeps. No standard rule is reliable: snapping a non-answer to its closest option credits answers never given yet buries correct ones, abstention discards answers stated in words, and likelihood scoring follows answer-token priors, so a single grade conflates capability with luck rather than measuring either. We release all code, labels, and the protocol, and argue benchmarks should report a non-compliance rate and at least two rules, not one unexamined grade.
